36 Preserving

To make Conserve of Roses with whole Leaves in it.

Take a peck of Damas Roses buds pick't and putt them into a
pott of a gallon or more, and poure upon them a Gallon of hott
Seething water and so cover the pott close, and lett it stans 8 days-
att the end of those days give it a good warme or two over the fire
and then take the Roses from the liquor, and add in like manner
the like quantity of roses again, and so do for the Same space
of ten or twelve times as is afore said. and in the end the roses
being clean taken away from the liquor, add thereunto six pods &
boile it till it coms to a syrup,  you may take half a peck of Da-
-mas roses buds, the whites being clip't from them, and putt them
together in an Earthern-pott close covered, and lett them stand as
it were a Stewing with a soft fire two or three houres, so that-
the leaves may be very well soaked, that you find them in eating
& taste very tender.

To make the Creame with dyett Bread.

Take your Creame thick and sweet, putt into it a good quantity of sugar
then take your Bread being Steept half an houre before in Damask roses
water & sugar; Cutt it in Square peeces & putt it in your Creame & sett it
on a soft fire, Stirr it allwayes for feare of turning, Boile it a good while
till it grow thick, Then putt in the yolks of 2 eggs well beaten, give it
one Boile & dish it up.

To make the Snow Creame

Take a botle of Sweet Creame and a gallon of Strokings, mingle them when
the Strokings are hott  & the white of 12 eggs well beaten with Roses water &
Sugar, have a good handfull of fine white stickes, if you can gett Basket withing
they will be the better & beate them all together with the whitings, they will be
the better, and as the Snow rises have a Scummer ready  to gather it of, & your
dish with a white round peece of bread to lay your Snow upon; If you lay it-
Still on the Bread it will laye the more handsomly, Butt before it be as bigg-
as you will have it take a long sprigge of candied larkspurs or candied Marjoran
and stick it on the loafe, but for want of a long peece of Rosemary, then make
it up as you fancy.

To make Orange Creame

Take a pinte of Sweet thick Creame, putt into it as much sugar as will-
Sweeten it & 3 or 4 Oranges, roles broken in litle peeces, Boile the Roles & sugar
Very well in the Creame, then brake the white of 2 eggs & beate them very-
well, and putt into your Creame wheb it is of the fire, then give it a warme
or 2 and Stirr it continually one way, and putt it into your dish, & when it is-
cold, Eat it. Some takes only the Juice of 6 oranges & sett it on the fire, & when it is
hott putt to it 3 yolks of eggs well beaten and Sugar, & lett them have one boile, Keeping-
it Stirring all the while, & scum it cleane. & putt it into a dish.


Preserving 37

The Spanish Creame

Take new milk 2 or 3 gallons, Sett it on the fire, and boyle it as you
would doe for clouthed Creame, Sett it on 7 severall Basons, the next morning
take the Creame of it into a Scumer, that the milk run cleare away from it
the which Creame you may beate untill it beginnes to be thick, and So Season
it as you shall thinck fitt.

The Lemons Creame

Take a quarte of Creame or more as you please, Sett it on the fire, when
it boiles turne it with a little Juice of Lemons, but not to much, have a -
curse cloth & tye them up in that; and hang it up all night; Then take -
almonds finely beaten, and So laye a Row of fine creame and a Row of -
almonds strowing a litle fine Sugar between.

To make a Syllibubb

Take a pinte of Creame, half a pinte of white wine, half a pod of -
Sugar, the Juice of a Lemon, ( for want of a lemon a little Veriuce or Cyder)
you must take a whiske and tye a Lemon's peele to it & a Rosmary's -
branche, putt in Some musk or amber, sugar or confitts & beate it very well,
and as the froth riseth, lett it Stand a little before you take it of & soe lay it
into your Syllibubbs pott untill you have filled it up & soe eatt it.

A Lemon's Syllebubb

Take a pinte of white wine & squeese a Lemon into it & lett a -
whole one laye on it, putt in a good quantity of sugar, take a qaurte
of the best creame, Sweeten it Very well with Sugar, lett the white wine
stand on the ground in that you would have your Syllibubb made in; then
putt your Creame into it or poure it, and hold up your hands as high as-
you can, & poure it Slowly on, lett it Stand a while & it will be right.

To make Clouted Creame

Take a gallon of new morning Milk, sett it on the fire with some Mace
Cloves, cinamon a litle bruised, & lett it seethe till it have had wallowed, then
poure into it a quarte of Morningd Creame & keepe it with stirring till it have
had another wallowe to the topp of the pan; then take it of the fire, straine it
thorow a Canvas into an earthe pan as hott as you can, Doe not stirr it, butt-
sett it on a colf place 2 days and it will have a thick cloute upon it, then take
it of with a scumer being first wett in milke and lay it on a dish & straw it-
with Sugar & soe serve it.

To make Goosberries Creame

Take a pinte of goosberries before they be full ripe, putt them in a Skellett with
as much foure water as will cover them, Boile them & straine them & putt them into
your Skellet again with a quarter of a pod of Sweet butter, and when they boile poure
into them 5 eggs very well beaten together, stirr them continually till they be thick
as you would have it, then Season it & keepe it in you dish till it be cold, & then
serve it up.

Preserving

To lay a Foote in Snow

Take a quart of creame 2 or 3 blades of mace, the yolk of 6 eggs & ye
white of one, Beate your eggs very well, then take a manchet sliced round
butt very thin, lay it in a sylver dish, cover it over with scak & putt upon
it a quarter of sugar finely beaten, sett it to stew upon a chafing-dish of coales
your creame being boiling putt it upon your stewed sops, rising up the
sops with the baack of a sylver spoone in some few places as you think good
of the whites you must make good snow, and when your creame is thorow
cold lay on your snow & draw upon it the proportion of a man with
coloured flowers, & lace your man's dublet with candied violetts. The best
now is made with roses-water & sugar beaten into froth.

To make  Dish of Creame

Take thin chips of Bread and lay them inn the bottom of a dish,
Chip marrow and lay upon it then lay som corrinnts upon it & some
cinamon & sugar upon that & then a laye of bread again & of every
other thingns within 2 fingers of theDish's side; Then take some creame
& beate it with the yolks of 3 eggs & one white, and when they are well
beaten putt it in the Dish att the sides, so as it maay rise all the other things
& soe Bake it in an Oven.

To make a good Banqueting Dish.

Make a good tender curd with some good thick milk, and wring
out the whay as dry as you can; Then pound the curds in a mortar with
5 or 6 yolks of Eggs, Beate it very well together & straine iti thorow a
strainer & then season it wiith sugar & a little rosewater & so serve it
out on plates.

How to make almonds Butter ye best way

Take a pod of almonds & blanch them in a Bason of cold water & lett them
stand there a prety while, then putt them into a mortar & beate them very  fine
& ever as you beate them cast a spoonfull  of faire water into the mortar, and
so beate them still till they be very  fine then take them up and putt them
into a big dish & putt thereto 2 or 3 porringers of faire water, & lett them stand
a while being covred with a cloth (least any dust shall com into them) soe done
straine them thorow a faire napkin in a Bason of sylver or pewter, & still
keepe them covred, then take your strainedalmonds out of the napkin & putt them
into the mortar again & outt a porringer full of water to them & straine them to
the rest of your almonds milk as you did before, and likewise vse it ye third time
Then take your almonds milk and straine it into a faire pna being clean scowred
thorow a fine napkin, Then take a porringer full of ye milk & sett it by & putt a
prety quantity of salt in, & so lett it stand; Then putt the pan of almonds milk over
ye fire

39

Preserving

ye fire, and lett it boile twice or thrice vp, then putt in your porringer of
almonds milk that hath the salt in and lett it boile a pretty while after, then
take it of the fire annd take a faire napkin, & lett two hold it a broad & ever
with your spoon cast it on the napkin one spoonfull after another & lett the
whay run out, and when it is all cast vpon the napkin, then putt it together
with your spoon and bind it with a pack-thred and hang it vp vpon a naile all
night &  lett the whay run out, and in the morning take it in a faire dishe,
and worke it with your spoon, and putt a good dole of beaten sugar in as much
as will make it sweet with 2 or 3 spoonfull of Rose-water, &  worke it well together
and soe putt it vpon you rplate. See another way pag 53.

My Lady Veres Sugar Cakes

Take a pod of loafe sugar, beat it & search it, 3 pods of flower
the yolks of 5 eggs beaten with a quarter of a pinte of thick
creame; Warme this blood-warme, then by degrees as youo work it
putt in your butter whihc must be about 2 pods, then make it vp
in paste, roll it & crush it flatt & cutt it with a glass of the bigness
you would make your Cakes. It must be wrought Very stiff, there-
fore some flower will not taake vp full two pods of butter.

To make allmonds Jumballs

Take half a pound of almonds, blanche them & beate them in
an alabaster mortar with now and then a spoonfull of read-roses water
then take a quarter of a pod of doubel refined sugar beaten fine & searht
mingle it well together as for macsh-pans, then towle it out and tye them
in knotts & putt every one on a severall peece of paper & so putt them
in a stove where they may have a litle warmth then the next day
glase them wiht the white of a small egg beaten all to froth, putt a
little Musk and ember-greece into it the night before, Beate the egg
& sugar well together tiill it looks very white, then with your bodkin
lay it on the one side of your Jumballs, then putt them in a warme
stove & lett them drye, then take them out again and glaze the
other side, Both sides must be done the same day, the glazing fresh
made the second time, some putt the whites of 2 eggs beaten with ye rose water &
shred ye paring of half a lemon

Too make Blammane

Take a quarte of thick creame, and when it boiles vp, putt into it
the whites of 12 eggs well beaten, & keepe it stirrinng till it boiles
then take it oof the fire & season it with sugar in 3 spoonfull of Rose
water, and strayne it thorow a Canvsas strainer





To make a Cabage Creame my Lady Vere

Take at least 3 gallons of milk & a good pinte of cream &
better to every gallon of milk, sett it upon the fire, as it seeth drop
in some creame till you have dropped in a fourth part; then take it
& poure it out in divers pans and basons att least 8 or 10; When it is
Skinned and rowled take of the skin by one side, it will com of whole
hold it up that the milk may cleane dropp from it, then lay it -
upon a plate full of holes & fashon it with a stalke like a cabage
leave and lay it in wrinkles like a cabage leafe, then have have in -
readinels a porringer of rose water and a bunch of Rosemary, So -
sprinkle the creame skin upon a plate with the rosemary out of the
rosewater, then shave some sugar oer it; then take another skin -
of another dish of milk and lay it over the other, keep it heigh in the
midst & sprinkle it with rosewater, scrape sugar over it as you did
the first, and doe so by every one till you have taken of all the skins
of the milk, then sett on the milk, make it scalding hott and drop
in some creame as att the first, but never let it seeth but the  first time
when it is scalding hott poure it out as att the first, and lay all the
skins over the other and strew sugar & sprinkle rosewater between
every one till it is thick as a cabage, you may well scald the milk
3 or 4 times, when you have done, lay it upon a cleane plate, to
serve it in, this will not keepe but must be eaten the  same day.

To preserve green apricots when the stone is grown in them

Codle the apricotts & peele them and sett them in a skellet of warme water to green,
covring the apricotts when in the skellet to greene with wine leaves or apricots leaves, that
the water may cover the fruitt. Then preserve them with their weight of fine loafe sugar,
when they have been 3 or 4 days in the syrup you may trust out the stones with a
bookin and the apricots will looke very faire.
To doe them small before they be stoned they be done this way, butt they will not peele,
putt them into the water warme as above directed & they will be very green.

To Candy Oranges flowers another way

Pick your flowers and putt them in water as you pick them, lett them steep in water
all night being close covred, next day take them out of the water & lat them between two
cloths and with a Rowling pin rub them till the leaves looke cleare, and as you doe them -
putt them into fresh water and take but a few out att a time, for they will loose colour when
all are rubed, boile them in the last water, you must putt them in as you rub them then when
they looke white, then they are enough. Then to pound of flowers take 1 pound 3 quarters of fine sugar
putt iust as much faire water as will wett the sugar, then lett it boile & skim it cleane. Then putt in -
the flowers, but lett them be well dryed from the water in a cloth; then boile them tender, and the syrup
must be so thick that you think it will crust over. But to make litle cakes you must boile them
to a candy & drop them on paper.

To make Rasberries Cakes.
Take a pd of Rasberries a pd of sugar, make your Sugar into
a hard candy, and have in readiness your Rasberries broken & sett upon -
a fire to warme a little; then when your Candy is ready putt them together
lett them stand upon the fire till the Candy be well melted, then sett the dish
into the stove till the next day; then take flatt peeces of glasses, & starcht -
peeces of paper cutt into 3 corners upon the glasses, and lay your cakes on -
the other side of the glass; When they are a little dryed, lay 3 of them one
upon another & turne upt he egges a little, some putt read Corrints with ythe rasberries

To make Juice of Liquorish - Lady Willougby-

Take too pds of English liquorish & scrape it very cleane -
Slice it but not to thin, putt it into a Skellet with a gallon of Spring
water, lett it boile till more then half be consumed, then take it-
from the liquor & bruise it in a stone mortar, then putt it to te
liquor again, add to it a quarte of hysope water & and quarte of
read roses water, So lett it boile all togheter till it coms to 3 pintes
or a botle; then straine it out from your liquorish very hard
and putt it into a skellet that is a great deal less then the other
Lett it boil till it be as thick as papp, Be carefull in stirring it
that it burnes not, then poure it out, and lett it stand till it Be
cold, then putt it in a mortar & worke it very well with as much gum -
-dragon as a hazell-nutt, the gum must laye all night in Rosewater
then worke it up. looke another way page 151.

To make Marmelade of Apricots

Take your apricots & pare them, cutt them in halves, then Slice them in -
Slices as thin as you can & as long, then take to a pd of apricots 3 quarters
of a pd of doble refin'd Sugar, putt so much water to your Sugar as will wett it,
and when it is dissolved sett it on the fire, & lett it boile till it be cleare & -
allmost ready to heate, then as it boile putt in your apricots & stirr them so
continually that you be sure they boile not, and scum of all the froth that -
riseth very cleane, and when you thinck they are scalded enough, (which you
may discerne by the smell & taste) they may stand on the fire about a quarter
of an houre, if the fire be not to hott, & when you see them cleare, warme -
your glasses & putt them In by the spoonfull & putt syrup over them & they be
thorough cold putt them into the stove & let them stand a weeke or more, -
while they be candied on the top, then set them in a coole place but note
from the ayre of the fie, & if they keepe well one quarter of a yeare, -
they will keep 2 or 3 yeares. And this same way you may make Jelly
of peares, plumbs putting in the Juice & Slices together into a Candy Syrup
& soe lett them Scald.


Preserving

To make Conserve of Gelliflowers

Take a pinte of faire water, Gellifloweres water or Burrager water -
which you will, make it seething hott in a Sylver pott, then putt in -
as many clips gelliflowers as you can putt into it, So let it Stand -
close covred till the next day only with a doble paper; then Straine
it out cleare, Sett it on the fire no more, but take more Gellyflowers
clipt & bruise them in a Stone mortar & putt them into the liquor
not heathing it again, So lett it Stand till next day, the Shift it
again & putt in fresh bruised flowers, so doe about 3 times, Then
Straine it again and putt in as many more gellyflowers clipt &
weigh first the liquor & flwers & boile it till the flowers are pretty
tender, then take the weight of af the flowers & liquor in Sugar & -
putt it to it & so boile it to a Conserve, & when itis enough -
putt in 6 drops ofthe Juice of Lemons.

To preserve Corrints
Take the fairest bunches to every half pd a pd of Sugar, then take a -
good quantity of Corrints & set them on the fire till they breake, Straine the
Juice, & take the 3d part or more of the sugar & putt in as much
Juice as will make it boild, cleare Skim it & putt in the Berries &
some of the other Sugar, Strow all over, Sett them upon a raison-
-nable hott fire, & as it boiles Strow Sugar on them; when all te
Sugar is melted Skim it, & Take out the Currints; lett the Syrup
boile to be thick then putt it into the Berries.

To dry Apricots

After they are preserved, take them & putt them into hot water
then dry them very well, & Strow Searched Sugar on them & So Sett
them in a Stove.

To make Cheese Cakes.

Take 3 gallons of milk and make curds of it, Draine them well -
From the whey, then beate them in a mortar, with about a pd of -
butter, then Season it with more Sugar & Salt to your liking then -
putt in allmost a quarte of Creame, about 3 whites of eggs & 8 or 9 -
yoks well beaten & so mingle all these together, & putt it into thin
paste made with flower, butter, sugar & water, so bake them.

putt into 20 peppins a quarter of a pinte of Sack & half a pd of Sugar,
& the peels of 2 oranges cutt extreme small. Boile yor apples first in -

To make Ringoe Creame

Take 3 pintes of Creame, putt to it a quarter of a pod of new Ringoe
Slice them very thin then take a quarter of a pod of naples Bisketts, pare
all the browne of top bottom & sides, then slice them in putt all in cold, then
Sett it on the fire & lett it boile about halfe a houre but not to fast, when
you putt it into your Dish you must stirr it till it be cold.

To preserve Citrons.

ake your Cirtons Scrape of the spotts & pick it cleane, and cutt it into quarters,
take out the  meate. Then you must have two skellets of water on the fire, when it
boiles putt in your Citrons into one of them, & when it has boileth a while putt yr
Citrons into a bason of cold water till they be cold, then putt them into the other,
then putt cleare water intot he skellet from whence they came, and when it boiles,
putt your Citrons into cold water & then putt it into the hott, so doe 3 times, shifting ye
water. Then take the full weight of your Citrons in Sugar, putt some water to it & lett it
boile till you have skimed it well then putt in you Citrons & lett it boile 2 hours; then
take to every pod of Citrons a quarter of a pinte of the Juice, & putt to it your Citrons,
lett it boile and skim it, then straine the meat of the Citrons, and when it has boiled
till the Syrup be thick take it of &  putt in your Juice Jf it be not enough to your liking
add to it the Juice of a Lemon & some amber-greece.

To preserve Figgs.

hoose the fairest figgs and not thorow ripe, Boile your water
then putt them in & boile them very fast till they be tender; Then
peel of the thin skin, take their weight in sugar, dipe ye lumps
in water & lett it turne into water, again boile it till it haurs lay your
figgs in a pewter Bason & poure your syrup on them; then heate
them twice a day till the syrup beinne to Candy about th esides
of the Bason, then lay them on plates &  dry them in  stove.

To preserve Goosberries another way

Take your great large cristaall goosberries, when they rae att their
full grouth, cutt of tops & tailes and slit them down in the crest; take out
all the stones, casat themm in cold water as you bot them; Then take their
weight inn doble refin'd sugar, and have in readnness boiling clean water &
putt your goosberries in, putt thm oover the fire till the skin comes of, that
is till they will peel of, then cast them ito cold water agaian then wett 3
pates of your sugar with water iust to wett it and putt it over the fire &
;ett it boile, scum it cleane, putt in your goosberries, butt the water must be clean
run fromm them in strow some of the remainder of the sugar over them, make
them boile pretty quick skum them clean then putt in to  apod of goosberries
half a pite of Juice of goosberries & half a pod of doble refin'd sugar to that
then make it boile as fast as you can, then if it beginns to Jelly putt inn all
your sugar, make it boile 2 or 3 waarms more, then take it of skum it cleane
that putt them in hott in glasses or potts, take of some Jully with yr goosberries
So putt them in 1 or 2 together till you have filled your glasses; Sett them by
till they are cold the next day putt as much Jelly over them as will cover them
when that is cold, paper them & sett them in a coole place.

To dry angelica

Syrup & lay it in a thin candy  but iust to boil bp, Dry it inn you stove

To make cleare Cakes of Goosberries

Take cristall goosberries when they are att their full growth cutt of tops &
tailes, wash them, putt them in as much cleane water as will cover them & no-
more, Boile them quick as soone as they beginne to breake take them of ye
fire and putt them in a cleane linnen bagg, tye your bagg close & hang it up,
& lett the juice run from them ; To a pinte of that juice take a pod of -
doble refin'd sugar, mix them together, beat it scalding hott over ye fire -
then take it of & skum it cleane & putt it in your glasses, lett it stand till
they are cold, then stove them in a moderate stove, when they are candied
on one side turne them on sheets of time & sett them in again, when they
are dry enough lay them between papers.

To make paste of Goosberries

Codell your Goosberries, Breed them thorow a haire cive, to a pod of ye
pulp, putt a pod of doble refined sugar, putt half of your sugar to it, lett it
be beaten & sifted, Boile it up quick & skum it cleane, then take it of the
fire & putt in your other half of sugar, beat it again over the fire, but -
not boile, then take it of, skum it cleane, putt it in glasses and order it -
as the cleare cakes.

To preserve green apricots in Jelly

Take yr greene apricots before they are stoned, putt them in cold water
& codell them a litle, till they rufness will com of, scrape them gently till the
rufness coms of. then putt them in a brass skellett in cold water with a litle
Venigar in it, when they are all done, cover the pan close & sett them over a
soft fire & lett them simer till they are as green as you would have them, then

To Candy Almonds

They must be blanchet & slitt downwards in thin slices, and take -
doble refind sugar & iust wett it with half water & half orange flowers
water & boile it almost to a candy height; then putt in your almonds & -
lett them boile a litle, keep them stirring, then take them of the fire &
lett the candy run from them & take it out of the pan, then putt your -
almonds over a soft fire & keep them stirring till they dry, then keep them
for your use.

To make pretty Puffs another way

Take cheese curds, beate it very fine, and as much flower as curds
& stirr it in a dish that will hold a quarte & putt in 4 eggs a grated
nuttmengg & season it with a litle salt, soe lay it in round cakes upon
papers that are well buttred, so lett them in ye oven to bake, they -
will rise much, you may tie them if you please. Serve them warme
with butter sugar & sack if you will.

To make Lemons creame another way.

Take 20 eggs, leave out half ye yolks & beate them very well with -
2 spoonfull of rosewater, then take 4 lemons of an ordinary size
& scowre out the juice, so straine the eggs & lemons thorow a cloth -
into a skellet & lett it boile softly keeping it stirring a great while
upon coales till it pretty thick, then take it of & sweeten it & poure
it into your dish, lett it be cold before you eate it.

To make Spanish pap.

rice, one grain of musk or amber-greece, 2 sspoonfull of rose-water, sugar as
you like, almonds blanched & pounded as much as you please. Boile your creame
first & lett it coole, then mingle them together over a chaffing-dish of coales

To make thym creame

Boile 2 gallons of new milk with a large sprig of thym & the whole rinds of a lemon,
then as it boiles putt in by degrees a botle of sweet creame, & when it boiles again, putt in half
a pod of sugar, stirr it well, then putt in the iuice of a lemon, then take it suddenly of ye fire
& stirr it till it be half cold, then cover it close & lett it stand a day & a night, & so dish it.

preserving
to make a Foole
Take a pinte of cream, & when it boils put to it the yorks of 4 eggs well beaten, the thready being taken out, so keep it with stirring, putting into it sugar & rosewater, so empty it into your cream dish.

To make a Rasberries Cream
Take Rasberries & bruise them well in a dish with a spoon & put to them a little rose-water & sugar , then set them over the fire with mask and amber-green.
So soone as it is warme take them of and when it is cold lay it att the bottom -

A quick way to dry grapes, apricots or plumbs
Stone peele & pare such fruits as require it, scald them in a very thin
syrup when they are cold, take them out & drain them well & take to a pd
of fruits a pod of fine loafe sugar, being finely beaten, strow some of the -
sugar att the bottom of the dish, then lay your fugarruits on it and strow more
sugar so till all your fruits is in, reserving t most sugar to lay in on t top,
lett this stand 5 or 6 days, then draine them well from the sugar & drye them
you may make any thing with the syrup that you meane not to keep, your
syrup most be cold when you first putt in your plumbs, but for other fruit
it may be hott.

To make Ginger Cakes
Take a pretty quantity of Elicampana the roots of it & a root of angelica
& a Confry root scald them & make them very tender & peele them very cleane
& take near an ounce of the best white ginger in races & slice it and boile it a
litle in Damask rose-water till it is pretty tender then in a stone mortar beate
the ginger & all the roots to a fine pulp, take a pod of loafe sugar & wett it
and boile it to a high Candy to a sugar again then putt in your pulp & stir -
it on the fire without boiling till it be very well mixed, & then stirr it till it
be pretty coole then poure it on a pye plate, and when it beginns to be -
pretty hard cutt it into Lozenges & dry them in a stove & so keepe them.

To dry preserved apricots cherries or peaches.
When your apricots are preserved as in page 17 & 40. Scald them 3 times - #
letting them laye in the syrup every time till they be cold, the last time take them
out & lye them in stove dishes & store them att first with a gently fire, they
require 3 or 4 days drying; when you putt them in dishes to store putt a litle
syrup on them & with a spoon, doe so 4 or 5 times a day untill they candy. ~Att
first after your apricts are pared you should prick them which will keepe them
from shrinking, after your fruit is scalded boile the syrup again to a candy height
so putt it on a as above. Looke also another way page 43.

Jelly of John's aples to lay on Oranges
Take Johns apples pare them & cutt them a litle less than quarters, pick
the kernels out but leave the cores in, as you pare them putt them into
water to keepe their colour, putt for every pound of apples 3 quarters -
of a pinte of fair running water; lett it boile a pace till it be half -
consumed then take it of, lett it run thorow a litle flanell or cotton bagg
then take the full weight in doble refin'd sugar wett the sugar well in
faire water boile it almost to a candy height then putt the apples liquor
& 2 or 3 slices of oranges peeles & one of lemon together lett it boile pretty
fast, when tis half boiled putt to it from orange juice a litle & a litle -
of lemon & a litle warmed together You must not put to much jiuces for
then it will not jelly, when it is jellyed putt it to your glasses that hae
peeces oranges putt in before

Cheese's Cakes another way
Take a quarte of creame & 14 eggs whites & all being well beaten, then
stirr your creame & eggs well together in a skelet over the fire till it curds
as much as you desire, then take it of, putt in it a quarter of a pd of corrints
& sweeten it to your taste putting in Rosewater & nuttmeggs, you must all -
the whilte it is on the fire keepe it stirring, least it burn.

To keepe quinces 2 yeares
Take stale bear or ale, boile it with refuge quinces pairings & cores pretty strong
lett the liquor coole & then putt in your quinces you meane to keep, tye them close -
in a pott, you need not putt out the pairings. Some instead of bear makes use of water.

a quick fire, stirring it continually till it be melted, then draw the loaves, cutt of yr
tops, skim up the curds within, poure in the butter, so serve it.

A Goosberry fool.

Take a quarte of Goosberries & a quarter of a pinte of white wine
& stew them on the fire till the Goosberries be soft then take the yolk-
of 3 eggs & the white of one a good quanitry of sugar, strain them together
thorow a haire live.

To make Creame Tarts.

For paste take 2 quartes of flouer, a quarter & a half of butter, half
a pinte of creame, the yolk of an egg, mingle the butter & flouer cold, so-
make up the paste with cold water, this will make 3 tarts. Take your pepins
pare, core & quarter them; with a litle lard in fron draw some thin peeces of
preserved oranges peeles as litle as a pin, lay thee apples in quarters, putt as
much sugar as will keepe them moiste, when they have stood in the oven half
an houre, cutt up the lid & poure in the creame. You must take 2 quartes of
creame the yolks of 8 eggs, breake them every one severally into a spoon-
cutt it with a knive in the spoon & the skin that contains the yolk pull out,
beate the eggs well, putt them to the creame, sett them on the fire stirring yr
creame till it is scalded, then take it of & puu in as much sugar as will sweeten
it & rose-water, so poure it in, lett it stand half an houre almost in the oven,
If you will ice them after the creame is in with the white of an egg & rose-
water beaten well, wash the lids over with a feather, search sugar on it thinly-
the tarts must be drawn 3 hours & a half before tis eaten.

To make a Lump Creame.

Take 2 gallons of milk & sett it over the fire till it be hott then have ready
6 whites of eggs ready beaten, mix them with a litle milk then take your milk
of the fire & putt in the whites of eggs stirr it very well & poure it into 3 milk-pans
& lett it stand 2 days then take of tops and lay it in lumps as high as you-
please in the fashion of eggs to the number of 6 or 7 in a dish, putt some raw
creame to them with sugar & rose-water scraping sugar on the lumps so serve it
this is most the same as clouted creame page 37.

To make Quinces Puff

Take a faire quince & boile it tender & take the pulp of it, putt it in a Stone mortar
& beate it, sift your doble rein'd sugar & beate the white of an egg to a froth, grind your
quince round putting often your sugar by litle & a litle egg, but still grind it, till it lookes-
very white & getts up to the top of the mortar, then drop it on plates in litle drops, &
putt it into the oven, when any thing is draw out, when they are enough they will be crisp,
are ripe, you need not scald them.

Preserving

Almond's Cakes. another way.

Take, if you would doe but a few 1 half a pod of almonds that are
blanched , & beate them in a stone mortar, every foot putting in some juice
of lemons to keep it from oyling; when they are beaten exceeding fine, take
it in a porrenger, cutt litle skware peeses of paper & each paper butter it
very thinly over, then with a knife spread the almonds on this papers round
like a cake, but you must care to spread it every where alike & very thin,
So putt these papers on a cleane gridiron or some such thing over a feld-
coales, and when they looke a litle yellow on the edges they are baked enough,
while they are hott they will come off the easiest, when you have with pa-
tience gotten the of, sett them by, & the next day clip them smooth round
the edges, if you scrape the papers, you may use them again, & they are
better then fresh, you may use them to severall times. When you ice them
take your fine sifted sugar, wett it wish orange flower's water & bode it to -
a Candy heigh till it strings in the dropping, spread it very thin on the cakes,
with a knife & when you have done so, lay them one by one in the pan,
as you doe puffs, so bake them with fire a top & a litle att the bottom and -
when the sugar glisters it is enough, you should laye these cakes on cleane
white paper, so take them up & putt the rest in, and if your sugar is so thick
that it will not spread thin, putt a litle more water in & boile it up a fresh,
when they are allmost cold, with a knife take them gently of & lay them
or dry papers, and so doe the other side as you did that, If you doe nott-
take them of before they are quite cold, they will not com of att all without
breaking. When you beate your almonds, me thinck faire water is best to-
wett them with, you must bestow much time & paine in the beating of tm.

To dry Damsons or any Plumbs that are black. another way
Take 4 pounds of plumbs & one pound of sugar, then slitt the Plumbs in the
seame & take out the stones with a bodkin, (but you must not abate of the
sugar for taking out of the stones) putt your sugar into two quartes of water
and when your sugar is melted & it doth almost boile, skum it and putt in the
Plumbs, cover them close, take it of the fire & so lett it stand a quarter of an
hour to scald, then take them out & lay them one by one on a live & putt
them in the stove, the next day turn them on glasses, so turne them till both
sides are dry; Then dipp them into scalding water, dry them in a linnen cloth
stove them till they are dry, then scald them in water, wipe them & stove them.
So doe till they shine & looke blew & are not clammy, be sure you minde your
stove, that they be not to long a drying; Sometimes you must scald them in
water 8 of 9 times before they will be dryed enough, smooth them handso-
-mely as you doe them.

A Goosberries Foole. another way.

Boile a quarte of Goosberries with half a dozen spoonfull of water, as much Sugar
as you think fitt, so boile it all to mash till t'is thick. Eate it cold. You may take some
of this & having boiled half a pinte of creame & stirred in the yolks of 3 or 4 eggs, putt
your scalded goosberries to them & keep them stirring over the fire some time, but not
to boile.

To Keepe Corrints till Christmass so fresh that you
may preserve them then

Take your gardens matts & fasten Very close about your Bushes, on
the top & sides approved

To preserve or dry Corrints read or white another way

With fine sharp sizers cutt of their black eyes & stalks & with a sharp quill
stone them att the eyes, strowing some sugar on them as you doe them to keep
their collour then take their weight in fine doble refin'd sugar that is beaten &
searcht strow your sugra a tp of them & lett them stand all night, the next
day take some juice of corrints & putt it to them so boile them as pag. 43
If you would dry them, you must not cutt them of the bunches, but ston
them & tye 2 or 3 bunches together, so weigh them & doe as you did to the preser-
ved letting them stand all night in the sugar & the next day putt in noe Jice
scold them as they arae three times in a day for 2 days then take them out and lay them in glasses & stove them.

Lemon's Biskets

Take a lemon, grate it yellow & white to the meat, take this grated rind
& putt it in a mortar & grind it with sifted sugar to a paste & then putt in
as much white of an Egg as will lye on a six pence, So grind it a litle more
then take it out & with sugar mould it & flatt it & cutt it into what shape
you will, you must cutt them or else it will not rise; Soe lay them on papers
in a baking-pan with a litle fire att bottom & a good deale at top.

To make Spanish pap another way

Beside what is orderd page 47 you must wash some custards dishes with sack
havinng before scoured them with salt, you may putt the sack you renched them with
into your sak pap, then putt your pap ito your dishes. (some think if you turn
them out of the dishes while it is warme that they turn out best other says cold)
So stick them a top wiht almonds blanched & eate it cold with bread.

To keep sweet-meat from moulding

Boile some water & sugar together, & dip paper in it & lay it close to yr sweet-meat

Rice's Custards

Take your rice, beate & sift it fine, then take whites of eggs, beat it to a froth
& take it in a spoon vp & putt it in another thign, so doe so long as as it will froth
then mingle that froth, & that that will not froth together & beate it vp so putt
in your rice & sugar & some rose-water stirr it well together, Sett your creame
on the fire & maake it boile vp then stirr it in this rice & boile it Keep it stirring
least it stick to the bottom till you thinck it thick enough, so poure it into your
Custard's dishs. If you would have them looke read take cochenill beate it fine
& putt juice of lemons to it straine it & poure it in your rice before you putt it
in your creame, as much of it as you think will colour it. If you would have it
greene outt in only juice of spinage to your rice.

To preserve cherries in Jelly

Stone them & weigh a pod of cherries & a pod of sugar & a lay of
cherries & a sugar till the most of your sugar be in; then take 3 pintes
of corrints pickt & straine out the juice & add half of this liquor to your cherries
so gently boile them & when they are well skumed putt in the rest of yr juice
& sugar adding in a full quarter of a pod of sugar or more becuase of ye currints
thus lett them gently boile, till they will Jelly, keepe them well skumed then
take vp your cherries without the syrup into a basom & with a bodkin putt
them one by one into the thing you keep them in, Boile your syrup a litle more
& putt it to your cherries, Keepe a litle out to putt on the next day

To make Barbaries Cakes

Pick your Barbaries, putt them into a pott, putt that pott into boiling water
when thhey arae enough rub them thorow cive; Beate & search your sugar, thhen
take your barbaries, sett them on a fire to drye; when it is very dry, wring in

Plumbs Creame

Take your plumbs & pare them & cutt them of from the stones then slice them
Very thim; then putt them into a Tankard, & sett them in a pott of boiling water
So lett it boile, till it Jellys then take it out & beat it with sugar & rosewater. Then
take soem creame & putt somelarge mace into it & boile it well then stirr it till
it be warme & putt inn as much of your plumbs suff as will thicken it, beat all together
Serve it cold.





Preserving

To make fruits bisketts. Mrs. Hux

Take the ripest apricots or plumbs you can get, putt them into a gally pot in a skillet of water, let the water boile till the plumbs are tender then put them in a white dish + pick out all the stones + skins, then straine the pulp + through a strainer of canvas that is thin, put 2 or 3 spoonfuls of the first running away then take 3 ounces of the thick + put it into a pott with 4 ounces of double refined sugar beaten + search through a lawn cive, thentake the whites of 2 eggs, beat them to a froth + put to your pulp + sugar the quantity of one of your whites. Beate it all together half an houre, until it comes to such a thickness as that you may drop it on a piece of white paper with a spoon without running into what fashion you please; then putt them into your stone keeping a moderate heat to till they are dry + then they will come cleare from the paper which they will be in 4 or 5 days. Keepe them in a dry place for your use. This is for your read bisketts. For the white you must pate + stone your fruits before you melt them in a pott, this way you may take them of all sorts of fruits.

To Make Sugar Plates

Put gum dragon in steep with rosewater, when it is well steeped, straine it out + put in cinnamon in powder or what you please to give it a taste, so make it up to a paste with sifted sugar + rowle it out thin with a rowling pin + cut it in what shape you please. It dryes presently with a little heat.

To Keep Gooseberries all the year

Take them full grown but not ripe, pick of the tops + tailes + put them into glass bottle fill them full, cork them well + put them into a kettle of cold water, salt them on the fire not to hot at first by degrees boile them a while thill you think the gooseberries are almost scalded, then take them + let the bottles stand in the water till the water is cold then take them out + let your bottles be fastened well either with waxsoft or leather set them in a cool cellar.

A Creme Sister Maries

Take half a pound of marmelate of apricots or preserved apricots or any fruits, the whites of two eggs new laid beate this an hour and a half till it be very thick + white then heap it upon servers

To Keep the Juice of Oranges or Lemons a year or 2

Take your Oranges , squeeze out the juice into a sylver tankard + put as much loafe sugar as you think will take of the extreme sharpness, put it in a lamp so let it stand till the next day and if it is not fined enough for it will looke like rock-water if it be cleared as it should be putt in more sugar and so doe as before till it is well cleared, then bottle it

Baboons

Take 7 eggs, leave out 4 whites then take half a pound of fine sifted sugar, beat your eggs in an earthen basom and of your sugar and put in flower by degrees still keeping it beating till you have made it thicker then hastye pudding lett your oven be heating all this while and you must beate it till it goes into the oven let your flower be very dry then butter some papers very thinly over and spread the stuff indifferent thin on the papers in a long square, so put them into the oven on thin plates and when the edges beginne to looke browne, you may guess them enough; then take them out gently with -



with a knife, and put them on wooden blocks to bow them, so sett them into the oven again to harden; so you may put another sett on to harden after you take them of the blocks, put them on some flatt thing + toward night set them into the oven for all night; you may butter some thin pans + make littlle cakes of some. If you please, you may ice these things with a cleare or thick ice. Keepe them dry, reserve some of your weighted sugar to beat in at the last, put in att your first beating some orange flowers water.

Conserve of Roses another way

Take a pinte of the juice of roses + put therein as many clipped rose buds as it will throughly welt + let it lye 24 hours, then put in a pinte of rosewater + boile it with as much sugar as all weighted.

To dissolve amber-greene

Take your amber-greene + with the juice of lemons grind it upon a stone for that purpose and if you thicken it be to thin, put a little powder of sugar to it + grind it till you see it all dissolved, putt it up close + keepe it for your use

To Preserve Green Grapes Mrs Edwards

Take the Fairest grapes before they beginne to fourn, pull them from the bunches and leaves on the stalkes, sett them over a quick fire, and scald them till they beginne to tender, then pull them, slitt and store them as you doe them sett them on a gentle fire dose covered till they be greene. Take to a pound of grapes, a pound and a quarter of double refined sugar half a pinte of water. Make the stirrup and skim it clean, putt in the grapes and boile them a pace till they are clear strong sugar finely beaten. When the grapes are cold take them out of the stirrup and boile it a little more if it be thin before you putt it to the grapes.

To Preserve green Apricots

Take a pound of the fairest apricots in the midle of June pate them thin, putt them in a skillet of boiling water till they are green, cover them close and set them on a gentle fire; take a pound of double refined sugar and a pinte of spring water. Put half of the sugar into the water make it seed + skim it cleare; putt in the apricots boile them close covered till they open on the side. Take them out of the stirrup, and store them fill them up with sugar and close them up again, put in the rest of the sugar.

To preserve ripe Goosberries.

About the 20th of Iune take the largest Dutch Goosber-
ries, before they beginne to turne the color, Cutt of the

To dry Quinces.

Quarter faire summer quinces, and thro them in water
take their weight into sugar, make it in sirrup with -
water enough to cover the quarters, & boile them gently,
till they are tender; putt in by degrees the rest of the
sugar, and cover them close, till they begin to looke of
a pale read; boile them till the sirrup be thick; lett
them stand a weeke, then lay them a draining all night
lay them on glasses and sift sugar on them, and sett
them in a stow not to hott, turn them on a day with
sifted sugar.

Preserving.

To preserve Oranges. whole.

Pare the best Sevill, pare them thin or grate them,
make a hole in the top, boile them tender in Spring water
then putt them in cold water 24 hours, lay them in -
a cloath to draine; Clarifie double the weight of good
pouder sugar, putt them in and lett them stand 2 daies,
boile them over a gentle fire half an houre; Sett them
by 2 daies & boile them again a quarter of an houre,
when the syrup is thick to drop putt them in potts
and when they are cold paper them close.
If you dry them you must take out the pulp with
your fingers when you take them out of the boiling water
att the hole in the topps, and putt them in a sirup of the
weight and half weight of sugar, they must be dried out -
of a syrup made with loafe sugar. the best time for them
is in Iannuary.

To preserve Citrons peeles.

The best white Citrons att the same time you doe Oranges
Scrape and roube them very cleane; cutt them in what pieces
you please, boile them over a quick clear fire; have -
ready their weight in sugar clarified; when they are -
tender, putt them in the sugar before they are cold, boile
them till they are clear, So boile them 3 or 4 daies -
once a day, adding half the weight more of good loafe Sugar
when they are cold, keep them close in pots or they will
dry out of the same sirup.

To preserve White Grapes.

Take the larges white grapes before they are to ripe
pick of the smallest one that grows between them, stone -
the largest in bunches, sett them over the fire swimming -
in cold water, let them stand till they look pale, they must -
not be tender in the water; the next day putt them into -
a thin Syrup, & let them stand 2 dayes, then Sett them -
over.

Lemons Creame

Take 4 large lemons, chip them thin, mince the chips, put
them into the iuice, lett it stand 2 or 3 hours. of 7 eggs take
out 3 yolks, beate them well, putt them to the peels with a
porringer of faire water 4 spoonfull of Orange flower -
water, Stirr it together and straine it thro a cotton, Sweeten
it with a double refin'd sugar, musk or ambregreese to yr
taste, stirr it over a chafing dish of coals, till it begin to -
be thick as creame, then dish it, it will keep a day or two.

How to make a fresh cheese or hedgehoge to
eate with Creame.

Take half a pinte of Creame and two eggs and beate
them and putt them into the Creame witha  spoonful
of sower Creame, and putt it altogether into a skellet
and sett it on the fire, and stir it continually & lett
it boile vntil it be turned to curds, then take it of the
fire and poure it into fresh Cheese pan, and lett the
whey be cleane drained from it, then take a good handfull
of almons and blanche them and beate them very smal
with rosewater, then put the curds to them and some
sugar and grinde them well together, then putt it to
the dish of creame, or make it on the fashion of a Hed-
gehogg & stick it with blanched almonds sliced.

To make Jelly of pippins Mes Christians way

Take 12 fair pippins pare and slise them coare them into a quart of faire
water and boyle tham a pace till the water be boyled to a pinte then str=
ain itt through Canuis and put to that 3 quarters of a pound of duble refined
suger Iuice of 2 Oringis & one Lemon pare the rine of the Lemon uery thin see
th itt in 2 or 3 waters till the bitterness be out of itt than cut itt into littel
strings then boyle the Iuce of pippins and suger put gather till you haue
scumed itt Cleane then put in the slised Lemon pill & boyle that neare
a puarter of a houre then put in the Iuce of Oring and Lemon according
to your tast and if you like a littel amburgrece & musk put itt in a tiffany
and boyle itt a littel in itt whan itt will Jelly one the spoon itt is enough you
may then put itt forth into glassis and keep itt for your use

To Preserue Wallnuts White Lady Mary Bertei

Take the walnuts 3 weeks after midsumer & pare them to the white as
fast as you pare them put them in faire water & keep them down un
der watter boyle them in the 2 watters & when you have paird them to
every pound of Nuts take a pound of suger & 6 ounces with near
a quart of watter when the watter & the suger is clean scumed putt
as much of the surrup as will couer the wallnuts let them boyle as
fast as the can & as the surrup wast put in more tell the are tender
when the are tender & near done put in a quarter of suger then
take them up in a cullinder & with a bodkin put Candid Cittorn
the next day giue them a boyle it must all be don with all speed

A Cordial Water against Infectious diseases as
plague. Small pox. Measels. Burning feavers. It is
good also to remoove any Venom or Surfeit &
to Restore the Spirits.
Take read poppies. read roses flowers. read sage. fennell.
Buglose flowers & leaves. Balme. Burrage flowers & leaves.
Comfreys flowers & leaves. Marygolds flowers & leaves. Tormatil
leaves & rootes. Scabius. Egrimony. Bettony. Dragon Wormwood
Celendine. Angelica of each of these one pound. Fetherfew.
Mugworth. read pimprenell. Dentdelyon. hartsease. hartstonge
Maydenhaire. liverwort Rososolis. Wild thime. Mother thyme
Burnett. Woodsorrell. Hysops. Rosmary tops. Lavender of each
of these half a pound. Mint. Rue. Elicampana rootes of
each a quarter of a pound. Liquoris & Gallingal of each
half a pound; Pick your hearbs cleanr & weigh them,
shred them smal with a shoping knife; Scrape & shie -
your rootes. Add to this Garroways seed one pound, Coriander
seeds sweet fennels seeds. any seeds of each half a pound. One
pound of both sorts of Cardamon seed.
Steep all these in fourty five quartes of white wine
you must have sweet earthen potts to infuse them in,
and cover them close for 2 or 3 days then putt in 4 pods
of raisins of the sun & 4 pounds of figs cutt or chop them
smal, lett all these be stirred once or twice in a day for
six days. Then you may beginne to still them in a cold
still, close the ioints of your still with a paste of rie -
meale. this water must be given to a man or woman
a wine glas full att a time, to a child less. you must
not eate or drink for 2 hours after but if you be
dry you may drink some of the samllest water. You
must keep the first by it selfe and also the second &
third running.

Aqua Mirabilis

Take two quartes of White Wine. one quarte of Aqua Vita -
one quarte of sack. the Juice of Salandine & Balme, Mint-
of each a pinte. one great handfull of Mellilot flowers -
small shreed. jubes. settmess. Gallingall grains. Nuttmegs of each
the weight of a sheling. Cinamon y.e weight of two shelings
Bruise all these spices small, then lett them infuse in an
earthen pan all night. the next morning distill them with
a gentle fire. Draw one quarte of the first water, and -
then of the second and third if you please, the last you
must spend first. putt into every quarte of a quarter of a pound
of fine safe sugar, as much pouder of safran bound up-
in a cloth as will lye upon 3 pence.
This water is good to preserve the lungs and to cause
a good stomach; In summer drinke one spoonfull fasting
in winter two.

The properties of the afore said water

The afore said water is restorative of all principal members
a preserver of all pestilential diseases as the palsy, dropsy
spleene, yellow or Black lander, for all agues hot or cold, for
all manner of swellings, sorrows, melancholy, or flegme, It
strengthen and confort all the vital spirits or springs, of ye.
Braines heart or stomach by taking thereof 3 spoonfull att
a time by it self or in bear ale or wine & by putting a -
pretty quanitity of sugar, It help digestion, break, the winds
stop the laske and binds not, it greatly helps heart burning
& quickens the memory.
Take of this water 3 spoonfulls in a day, one at morning
one at noone & one at night.

A most Excellent Milk water for a Consumption
Take a gallon of new milk with a manchet sliced thin
& 16 egg with the yolks & while beaten very well together
& 4 or 5 blades of large mace and one nuttmeg sliced, one hand
-full of the tops of balme and as much of the tops of spirmints
and a handfull of sweetmajoran, a quartet of a pound of
Eringo's roots candid, as much of candid citron's peel & two
pennyworth of safran. Putt all these into a cold still, now
and then stirring, else t'will burn too, soe draw it of in glas
botles with a lump of double refin'd sugar in each botle
when t'is distilled stirr it all together in a Bason, and
heighten it with a little spirit of wine; and drink a quarter
of a pinte of it in the morning fasting and at 4 a clock
in the afternoone and fast an houre after it.
If you please you may add earthworms & snailes.

To make Orange or Lemon's water
Take a Hundred & twenty Oranges or Lemons and 4 gallons of
Brandy, cutt of the outer rings of them thin and putt them into ye Brandy
and lett them steep 24 hours; then stick them in a cold still, mix all
the waters together and when it is cold sweeten it with double
refin'd Sugar or white sugar candy, If you parfume it putt a grain
of musck & two grains of Amgregris together in a piece of tifany
& tye it to the mouth of ye. Still, & lett the water drop thorow it.
Some lett the oranges steep a full Month in as much brandy as will cover them, and then
add as much or more of the best sherry-sack and so still it.

Triacle Water
Take Burrage flowers; Marygold flowers; Buglose flowers; of
each 2 ounces; Rosmary flowers and tops 2 ounces; Cardus one
ounce; Angelica roots one ounce; Citrons pells once ounce; tormentill
roots one ounce; cyprus roots one ounce; Elecampana one ounce;
one pound of the best Venice triacle; Damask rose water 3 pints;
hartshorne one ounce; putt to these a gallon of strong white wine
and distill it in a cold still.

An Universal and Aprov'd Palsey Water
Take of Lavender's flowers stript from the stalks as much as
will go into a glass of 2 gallons, and putt on them the best
strong proofe spirit of wine with Brandy extraordinary good.
then take flowers of sage; Burrage; Century; Mallilot, flowers;
Agnus castus; Marygold; Pomgranatt; Orange; flowers of Camo-
mille; Thime; Hysope; Sweet Marhoran; of each too moderate handfull.
These are to be infused in Malmedie or lachrima Christi 6. weekes.
Then putt Cardamanes, cabebes, cinamon, nuttmegs, mace, and Ginger,
ye. Yellow of citron pills of each of them 2 ounces. These are to
be infused likewise in pure spirit o wine, these putt together
one Month; then take Balme, Mother-worth, violets, Cowships--
Maiden-haire, Ditany of Creete, Spikenard of each 3 handfull,
next lignum aloes, Gum Arabick, Saiapelum, Opopianax, B. Dellium
Radir Serpentaria, Giacum chips, Mummy, Sparagda stone of -

of pearls, corall volatile, salt of Ameber, musk and ambregris

of these an ounce, those putt into a glap with 3 quarters of Aqua
profiladua, which must stand in the same 3 Months att least in
which time it will be exhaused to a very small quantity, but
that which remains will retaine the physicall quantity of ye
while, These putt all together; you must still about Michael mass
but be sure to keep them very close (it must Be in a balneo maria
for if the spirits are not kept close. the whole is spoiled) but
the least care will prevent that.

Rules in Stilling

You must begin with a slow fire att first & so grow hotter keep a
constant heat to make all waters good & strong, you must lute your still
with clay & brine mingled together (except you still in a glasse still) & wett a peece
of bladder & lay it vpon the nose of your still & the glasse that receiver the water to
keepe the spirits in, lett the glasse stand in cold water and as the water warmes laid it
out & putt in cold. Keepe wett cloths vpon the cape of your still.

Another Snailes Water
Take a pack of garden snailes shells & all & fry them in a
pann, so put them ina stone mortar & bruise them shells
and all, then take a quarte of earth-worms,slice them down the
middle & scorore them with salt and wash them clene. Make your pott
Very clene,then putt intow handfulls of Angelica leaves & stalks,
and put them in the bottom of the pot, then take two hanadfull of
of Salendin & lay it upon the top of that, then take a quart
of rosemary flowers , two handfulls of Bitony, two handfull of
Egrimony, two handfull of bearesfoot, Read Docccckk roots, Barbary
tree bark and wood sorrell of each of these two handfull &
half-herbe grass half a handfull penecuick and Turmerick of each
one ounce, staffron well dryed and beaten to powder Y.e weight of
6 pence. When all these are putt into he pott, putt in y.e worms
and snailes and last of all putt in 3 gallons of the strongest Ale
you can get and cover the pott so close that no ayre can get
into and so let it stand in the place where you mean to
still it in 24 hours. In the morning when you putt fire to it, you
must open the topp and putt in 6ounces of very good cloves beaten
to powder, 6 ouncs oof hartshone shaved, but but stit not the pott att
the bottom, but pressthem downe. The set on your Lymbeck,
and make it fast with a [iece of rie dow & then make the fire
under it & draw it very gently; ye first water is ye strongest
& must be reserved by itselfe, the other waters are weaker and
must not be mingles with the first until they be colld.  The water
must be given to the patient in the morning, who must fast
two hus after 7 not sleepe upon it. It may ne given between
meales or 2 hous before supper; you may take a spoonfull of
the strongest water and 4 spoonfull of beere and when you give
teh samllestwater ye same quantity in beere and put some sugar
into it. This is good against the Iander, Consumptiom or any other
weakness in the stomach liver or heart.

How to make Spiritt of wine
Take Lees of wine and distill them in a Lembeck & [th]n take
& putt it into a glasse - still and then sett it into Balnes- Maria as long
as it distilles with long streames oyly it is a good Spiritt then the second
is a weaker Spiritt take the strongest and distill it again with a soft fire
so that it doe but breathe and as long as it runs oyle it is perfect good
when you have a pinte or quarte of this spirit to know whether it be
all Spiritt or not you may pour some into a Sylver Spoons & sett it on the
fire & it will burne so long as there is any Spirit in it. And by this you
may know what quantity of good Spirit you have. Then take some of
your salt of tartar to a pinte 2 or 3 dragmes to a quarte twice as much
it being putt in & shaked together so [th]e salt be dissolved and lett it settle
one or 2 houres and you shall see the spiritt swim aloft and the fleame
on the bottom sensibly parted. Pour that softly that swims at topp into
a glass. Body leaving some 3 shaves breath before you come to [th]e. Separation
Distill this once over again. And here you have Reclarifyed Spiritt of wine
better then if it were 7 times stilled over. Then you may take the ither
that remains in the botle and distill it soe long as any good spirit comes
and keepe that for your use by it selfe and selt the bottom in a Skargell
glasse on hott sand or hott ashes to vapour away and then you shall have
your salt again
To make Spirit of thym, Rosmary, Sweet Masjoran or
and other Hearbes
Take a good quantity of any of the hearbes & fill a Rundlet with it
then putt as much water as will cover it over to every gallon of water
putt 6 spoonful of honey stirring the water & honey well together and
before you putt it to the hearbes. Close up the vessell and lett it stand
a Month or better. Then putt it into a still and distill it with a gentle
fire, that which come first is the best to be distilled in a glass still is [th]e
best. Is will worke like unto drinke, therefore your Rundlet must have
some vent.
To make Spiritt of Caster
Take of Calamne 4 ounces of nipe half a handfull of dills seed as much
cloves 2 dragmes of oranges peels the white being parted from them2 ounce
of allnuts blossoms half an ounce of Rosmary flowers a good handfull of
the tops of sage half a handfull of casterum one ounce Distill all this in a
Lembeck. To every ounce of Caster and to [th]e aforesaid quentity you must putt
1 quarte of the best white wine & soe distill them. This water is of excellent
use for any that is troubled with winds or swounding fitts or have a weake
stomach and cannot digest their meate. Weomen with child may take this [th]t
are troubled with fainting fitts. is doth much revive the spirits.

likeing. So fill it into great glasses and when it is well setled you may fill the

An approuved water against the stone.

Take Saxifrage, pelletory of the wall, parsley, Mother juice of each
5 handfull, Radish or the quantity of them in Horsradishs, Lett
them be washed, sliced with the hearbs; Dry them in a cleane cloath and -
bruise them in a mortar; then putt both Radishs & hearbs in an Earthen
pott with 4 quartes of new Milk from the cow, cover the pott close, lett
them stand 24 houres, and then distill it in two stills with a soft fire,
It must be stilled in the midle of May. You must take it 3 mornings
together in each Month thorough the year either att New or full of ye. moon,
or oftner if need requires, when you take it mix 5 spoonfull of this water
with as much white wine with nuttmegg & sugar warme together walking
or using some moderate escercise after it.

The Citron's water, the Lady Newton's way.
ake 5 great citrons, pare the Rindes thin of and take the Rindes -
and the seeds cutt in small peeces; and steepe them together in a gallon of
sack twelve or 13 dayes in a close covered vessell; Then distill them in a -
glass still, & lett it drop on white sugar Candy. You may add some perle
& ambergreece if you please, and this is the true way of making citron's water

For Convulsions

Take Beanes when the bloome is fallen of & the beane is coming in the -

Another water for the stone.

Take in the latter end of June so much Saxifrax as being distilled will -
yeilde a Botle of water, then so soone as hawses be ripe take a peck of them,
& bruise them very well, then putt into them your water of Saxifrax & 3 quartes
of white wine and as much new milk, then take of parsley and of polipodis of
each 3 handfull, beate & bruise them as you did your hawses, then mix them all
very well together & so distill them so long as any water will distill.
This water must be taken att first 3 or 4 spoonfull with so much white wine, but
if you be occasioned to take it often, then take it without wine or with very litle
this will keepe 2 yeares in full strenght.

A stomach water

still it.

of pure honey. Putt all together into a cold still & so still them over a gentle fire. Take
the quantity of a quarter of a pinte with a spoonfull of oyle of sweet almonds 2 mornings
together twice in a Month.

For a Cough or Consumption

Take of read cow, striking, a quarte of choice muscadine 1. pinte,
of raisins of the sun, half a pod. of choice blew corrints half a pound,
of Dates 1 . ounce, choice cinamon half an ounce liquorish two ounces,
any seeds one ounce. Steepe all there in the wine & milk 16 hours
att least often stirring them then distill them gently in Balnes Maria.
The corrints and Raisins must be picked & rubbed but not washed,
the raisins stoned, the dates sliced, the cinamon & liquorish bruised
but not beaten.
The doze is six spoonfull to be taken first & last, however you may
as often as you please, & sweeten it with white sugar candy beaten as
sweet as you like it. the sweeter the better.

An excellent water for a Consumption & cough of ye Lungs

Take a runing cock and kill him, when he is almost cold cutt him abroad by
the back, and take out the intrals & wipe him cleane; then cutt him in quarters
& breake the bones, then putt it into such a still as you still rosewater with -
one quarte of sack on pod of corrints, on pod of raisins of the Sun, the -
stones being taken out, a quarter of a pod of dates, the stones taken out & ye
dates cutt small, 2 handfull of wild thym, as much arganum, 2 handfull of
pimpernell, as much rosemary flowers, buloses flowers & burrages flowers
of each 2 handfull, fennell rootes, parsly rootes the piths taken out, the leafes
of endif siccory, colesfoot, maiden-hairs of each one handgull, figgs anyseed
liquorish of each 3 ounces, Beate all these small, then take a botle of new -
milk of a read cow & still all these things with a still fire & putt into ye
glass that the water doth droppe into, half a pod of sugar candy beaten very -
small, one book of leafe gold cutt very small amongst the sugar, 4 grains of
amber-greece. Mingle the strong with the small & drink thereof 4 spoonfull -
att a time in the morning fasting, & an houre before supper, shake the glasse -
about when you drinke it. When the time of the year is that you can -
-not gett teh hearbs, you may still it without them.

A cordial water against Melancholy. DrWright

Take of Cowslips one pod of the flowers of Rosemary one pod of burrage
flowers as much, munchey rubarb roots half a pod. Buglose roots half a pod
peppins & raw quinces a pod Quices marmelad a pod hald a dragme of saffron,
slice the roots after they have been washed, then putt all these things into a
great glass & putt to them an equal proportion of white wine & black cherries
water, & stop the glasse & lute it close wiht paper, Then sett it in a horse dunghill
for 10 days, this sill it in a glasse still.

A Reciet to make good & perfect Aqua Vita of Ireland
Which wee call Vsqua-bah.
First brue a good hogshead of good ale, neither to Strong neither to Smal
lett it stand till it be 10 or 12 days old, then drew as much of the ale as will
fill your pott 3 quarts full, which you meane to distill for wort for your
Aqua Vita, Draw your wort so long as you feel it have any strength, then
take the same & putt it in a ferking close stopt that noe fire may com in, -
least it loose its strenght In like manner draw the rest of your ale, when -
you com to the bottom stirr it and take ground & all, as you draw your wort,
putt it into your vessell having great care to stopp it close. In distilling your
wort keep but a meane fire, for a hott fire putteth your pott in danger.
Your still being sett vpon your pott, mouth, close it either with dong or clay -
looke well that the pipe of your stillatory be cleane or not stopped, wash it well
& blow thorow before you sett it on. When your wort is stale 10 or 12 days in
Summer will be suffiecient, least it lower & so loose its force, in winter it matter
not the it be a month or 6 weeks old, new wort never gives to good a taste
as stale & old. Fill your master pott with the wort you propose to make your
aqua vita with, it were very good your master pott did contain 8 gallons,
of wort, putting to the said quantity 112 ounces of good anyseeds well beaten
in a mortar & 4 ounces of liquorish well bruised, then close up your pott,
with the cover being made fast att both ends by some devise from ye cares
of the pott, to hold the cover faster downe putt some stones vpon it to keep
it the better, Lett the hole in the midst of the cover whereon the still is to -
stand, be open, lett your cover be of wood of the thickness of the brink of your
pott. When your wort is hott ready to still you may easely perceive by the
breath that will issue out of the hole, whithdraw all the fire from the pott, you
must have a great care when you see the breath first com out the pott, to -
draw all the fire away, for otherwise it endangereth to sett all ye fire about it.
Putt a stick in att the hole & stirr your wort well, then clap on your stillatory
of stopping it very close with dung or clay then fill the stople of your still full
of water. When you feele the foot of your stillatory hott, draw away
the fire from the pott & hold a dish under the Spoute to See how the water cometh
sontinue it with a moderate fire, and as your water waxeth hott vpon the Stople
of the Stillatory empty it & putt it cold. When your water cometh thin & cleare
then putt a pott under the Spoute of your still, putt therein a litle quantity
of cloves mace & cinamon stamped & a few musk confits whole & one ounce of
turnesot, then cover your pott with a cleane linnen cloth tyed about the pott -
mouth, lay on it one quarter of a pod of any seed well beaten & 2 ounces of li -
liquorish well stamped & one ounce of turnsole. Draw your your wort as long as your Aqua
Vita will being cast into ye fire, which you may proove, taking a Spoone
& holding the same under the spoute till it be full, & then throw it into the fire.
Lett

Stilling.
Lett it remain in the pott till the Aqua Vita be cold for it will take
his colour the better & then putt it vp in a botle.
If your Aqua Vita be hott in the mouth, putt a stick of liquorish bruised
into the botle, and it will make it mild.
If you would not have your aqua vita So Strong & hott in the mouth then putt noe the cloves in it at all.
To make Oyle of Balme
Take Turpentine one pod & putt it into a Body of glasse & warme
it att a soft fire, vntill it Be luke warme, & putt therein by litle & litle with
Stirring it, the pouder of frankicense 5 dragmes, of lignum aloes, Mastic
cloves, gallingall, cinamon, mace, quibibus of each one dragme all -
poudred together, gum of a figgs tree six dragmes made into pouder,
mix them all with the turpentine, make luke warme as is afore said,
andwhen it is well incorporated together, sett on the head of ye Lymbeck
& lett it be well pasted and sett it on ashes & putt under it an easy fire,
when it begins to distill Lett it drop away a litle, for the first drops
are woeth a litle, and putt the glasse to the nose of the Lymbeck very -
close & linke them very well together that noe ayre goe forth, for them ye
Vertue of it goe away. Know that the first will come a white & cleare -
water which keepe by it self; then will come the second water of an -
heavely ayery colour, which keepe by it Selfe; then recieve the third which
is best which will be yellow & thick as honey. the first of these is called -
the water of Balme; the second the oyle of Balme; the third is called -
Balme artificial; this proofe to all examinations is, that you take a -
drop of the same, & lett it be putt in a vessel full of water as you doe -
of natural Balme, for the said drop will goe to the bottom of the water
and it will stand whole there as though it were pure Balme; this is of
great verture & of some is called the mother of Balme; the first vertue
if it is that it burnes, the second is that if you washes your nose & face
therewith & that 3 times a day, it cureth & helpeth the Rheumes, it cleared
the sight, it conforteth the Sinews; and if you washe therewith the binder
part of the head, then it conforteth the memory & all ye vertues of ye minds
it procureth appetite, it conforteth the Stomach, it helpeth a Stincking Breath
as well coming from the stomach as of the brains, If you take 4 or 5 drops
once a day in a litle wine. If a cloth dipt in the same, compass a Toade
or Serpent it will dye, altho they touch it not and so it doth in all kind
of cold venoms, as the biting of a Toade or Serpent, for it healeth if you
wash it often with it, & it heales dryes vp any kind of Scabbs or Tetter
quickly if you washe it therewith twice a day, and if you putt 3 or 4 drops
of this into the eare & suffer the same there as long as you can it helpes -
all noyse and sounding in the eare; If you mix therewith an equall proportion
of the

Stilling

of the uice of Iermander, & putt 3 or 4 drops therof 4 times in the day
then it cureth all deafness of what cause soever, it cureth watery & bleered
eyes; It cures clarifies restrains & comforts, if you wash them therewith
twice or thrice a day all Imposthumes and ulcers & other superfluitees, it

of the teeth, all wounds in the head tho they be deepe are cureth with ye same

Cankers, nolime tangere, the Kings evills & every other eating soares if
you washe the same therewith twice or thrice a day. It heales all kind of
goutes if you wash or anoynt them therewith so that they come of a cold cause
laying a linnen cloth dipt therein vpon the grieved place. It helpes all
stroakes either by falls or otherwise if you anoyn it. It helps ye hemoroids
& fundament that goes forth & makes it tarry in its place. It helpes the
Palsey & all weakness in the members. Know that it is most hott & of
such penetration that if you putt one drop thereof in the hand, by & by it
penetrated the hand without any hurt, and you shall not feele any swelling
of the feet or paine in the Ioynts if you anoynt the same therewith, it
will helpe if you dippe a linnen cloth therein & plaster it thereunto, Briefly
if you will vse it, it cures all cold disseases & griefs & all disseases coming
of a cold cause of flegme and corrupt blood, this was found in the Abby
of Elson in Lincolnshire & translated.

The Wallnutt's Water. called the Water of Life.
Take wallnus in the beginning of June; Beate the greene huske shells
and Kernells very well in a mortar, then still them in a still of lead, keepe
that water by its selfe. Then gather more walnuts about the midsummers
day, and vse them as you did the other & keepe that water by it selfe also,
Then about a fortnight after that which will be a weeke within July, gather
more wallnuts & use them as you did both the other keeping that also by it-
selfe. then take one quarte of each water & putt it together and distill it in
a stillatory of glasse & keepe it close stopped for you vse.
This water healeth all manner of Dropsey & palsey, being drunk with
white wine fasting. It is good for the eyes if you drop one drop thereof
into them. It is good to help the conception of a woman, if she drinke
one spoonfull a day in white wine. It will make one's face very fine if it be
washed therewith. It is excellent for infirmities within the body, driving
out of it all corruptions if it be drink moderately either in wine or water
It killeth worms in the body either drunk in wine or by its selfe. And if
wine has lost his colour, putt a litle Vyall full of this water into it & within
4 days it will rectify it again. Whoever drink of this water continually
shall live as long as nature may well continue in them.

Stilling.

To make Spirit of Clare.
Still a botle of Clare flowers water in a simple still, putt the
water in a depe galley-pott; putt to it a pack of Clare flowers first
bruised in a stone mortar; stopp your pott close & sett it in a pott of
water & keep it hott two days, but not to boile; then straine them out
and putt in as much more, & so doe 3 times, Then add to your liquor
a botle of good sack, and so putt it into a glasses still, & draw it off
with a soft fire; you may draw it vntill it is of such a strenght as
you like; then add to it as much white sugar candy (being beaten &
searcht) as will sweeten it to your taste, & laying in it amber-greece
& musk tyed up in a Tiffany; stop your glasse close, stirr it every day
vntill all the sugar be melted, soe lett it stand a fortnight, Then straine
it out & putt it up in glasses.

The Lady Liggin's her water.
The halfe a dozen pod of black cherries, and one handfull of prunellas
and one handfull of woodvine leaves & one handfull of Bryers leaves & one
handfull of strawberries leaves, Beate your cherries with the stones, & putt
all these hearbs together in a stone mortar; Then take foure quartes of
new milk & putt it to them & so distill them. Lett the patient drinke
half a pinte int the morning and half a pinte att night; you may take
more of the prunellas & Bryer then of the other.
A milk water for one whose water is blood, heats or sharpen
Take new milk from the cow as much as will fill an ordinary
Rose still, to every quarte you must putt in two whites of eggs beaten
very well before you putt them in. Then distill them gently, once in
halfe an houre lift vp the head of your still so high as to breake the
creame that will keepe the water from rising; Distill it till the milk
be thick like a thin costard. Then drincke every morning & night
or when you please a large draught of this water with fine sugar
sweeten to your owne taste, this never fade, you may take it as long
as you please. but when noe blood appeares leave out the whites of eggs
they are to cold for some stomach.

To make Rasberries wine white.
Take Rasberries and putt them into a cold still, and Distill them,
then mingle that water with white wine. which makes very good
Rasberries wine.

Rue water.
Take your rue of it be green & lett it lay 3 days, then pull of the leaves from ye great stalks
& cutt it small, so putt it into half white wine & half brandy, make your wine & brandy of a
reasonable thickness with your leaves, so putt it into your still & lute it up, & the next day still it.
Dry rue will still thus very well.
