Stilling
For the Spleene

Take balme of Scabius, betony & pimpernell of each a good quantity
a good deale of Venice turpentine amongst the hearbs, laying them in the
still, so still it alltogether. Drinck often of this water especially morning &
evening & it will cure it.
For the Stone another
Take a good handfull of read stinging nettles & as much wild thym, two
good handfull of bean pods newly gathered, a quarte of milk from the cow, ten
eggs shells & all brocken, worke them & the milk well together, then take as much
white wine Venigar as will turne the milk well but not to much. Mix all well
together & distill it in a moderate still with a gentle fire. When you fear the
fitt is com take 3 or 4 spoonfull cold to prevent it, tis best att the new & full
Moon for prevention, however it is good at other time when you are ill.
The Lady Allins water
Take of read Sage, rosmary, dragons mugwort, wormwood, pimpernell, egrimony
rosa-solis, carduus benedictus, scabius burnet, sinfield, balme rue celendine of each
one handfull or other like quantity by weight, wash them & shake them in a cloth
and then shread them and poure on them a gallon of white wine, with a quarter
of an ounce of gention's roots and as much angelica's roots, lett it stand 2 days -
close stopt in a deep pott. So distill it in an ordinary still & keep it close:
the doze is 2 or 3 spoonfulls to a child & 5 or 6 to a man or woman. It is excellent for Conulsions fitts.
To wash the face after the Small pox
Take all the Calves feets with the haires on, pick them & rub them very cleane, but not washe them, breake them to peeces, & lett them lye 24 hours in steepe in 2 quartes of white wine, then still them slowly: wash with this.
A water comparable to gold
Take a gallon of white wine, 4 ounces of gention, 24 ounces of century, stamp
them together, putt the wine to them & lett them soacke together 5 days. Then distill
them in an ordinary still, keepe the water in a close Vessell. When you use it,
take 2 or 3 spoonfull morning & evening. This water preserves the body from -
impostumes, makes good colour, Resist plague, avoides stufft stomack, repells &
putts away all watrish humours, breaks the stone in the reins, purges the belly
& spleen, cures purges Collour & correct blood, it cleares Sight.

The Lady Huetts Cordiall water

Take of anyseeds, fennells seeds, Carroway's seeds, parsly's seeds of each 2 dragms
of Spicknard, elecampana roots dryed, melliots flowers Calamus aromaticus, Cardamon
lignum Aloes, rubarbe sliced thin, gallingall, Veronica of each of these 2 dragmes. of
Juniper's berries one dragme; of aromaticum rosarum 3 dragms. of zodiaria, quibibus
grains of each 2 dragms; China's roots one ounce, Sassadras sliced half an ounce;
hearts-horne 2 ounces. Washe all the hearbs & swing them in a cloth till the
water be well drained from them, then lay them thin upon a table to drye better,
after Shread them small & putt them into a earthen pott, & putt thereunto as much

To Open & purge the Liver

Take a pinte of white wine, & the weight of six pence in rubarbe, slice it thin
and putt it into the wine, & lett it steepe all night, then divide it into 3 parts, & soe
take it 3 mornings fasting.

A present Remedy for a paine in the Side

Take a quarte of Ale & boile it & Skim it, then putt into it a peece of Rossin
a little bigger then a wallnutt, lett it melt in the Ale, stirring it all the while with
a cleane stick, when it is melted take it & sweeten it with sugar & give the party
pained half a pinte att night when they goe to bed, and as much in the morning
as hott as he can drinck it.

A Vomitt for an Ague

Take 9 spoonfull of read roses-water & 6 of the iuice of Oranges, make it -
Very thick with Sugar & when you perceive your fitt a coming, sett it on the fire,
and when the Sugar is melted in it, take it & drinck it of. these 3. should be putt in phy.

Another Snaile's Water
Take 3 gallons of the strongest Ale and two gallons of Sack, then take one -
peck of garden's snailes and make a fire of Chearcole, & sett a large driping-pan
on the fire, lett the snailes roast in it so long as they continue hissing, take them
out & bruise them & putt them into the Ale & sack with 8 ounces of harts-horne -
one ounce & a half of Cloves bruised, 2 good handfull of Celendine, 2 of Angelica's
leaves, and 3 quartes of rosmary flowers; Stop the Mouth of your Alembeck with -
the paste, and in the morning distill it upon white sugar candy. Keepe the first
by it selfe, & so the second & third. If you have skill in stilling it will doe very -
well in a rose's still.

A water for Convulsions fitts

Take Junipers berries one ounce. Bay-berries, half an ounce, Pyonys berries 6. dragms
Camphir 2 dragms, fine rubarbe one ounce; assa foetida 2 dragms. Rue water 3 quarts.
Beat all the Berries, Rubarbe Assa-foetida & Camphir, then mingle them with the rue
water & distill them in a Roses-still. But if you distill them in a glasses still you must
have 4 quarts of rue water. Before you beate e Camphir, rub e mortar with an Almond
To a child that has fitt, give a spoonfull att a time, 3 or 4 times a day. If you only feare fitts
give a spoonfull every morning for a month together. To an old body give six spoonfull
in a morning for a Month.

Stilling

How to keepe Roses 2 or 3 yeares to distill.

Take a Rundlet, the one end being taken out, and strow a good handfull of
bay-salt in the bottom of that; then take some roses and bruise them a litle in a
mortar, and lay them a finger thick into your Rundlet upon the top of your Salt,
and then strow another strowing of Salt upon your roses, and so one of Roses and
another of Salt, untill your rundlet be full, remembring allwaies to bruise them
a litle in a mortar, and when it is full cause your Rundlet to be stopped up so
close as you can So that none of the liquor runs out nor take any aire, allways
remembring to turne your Rundlet once every weeke or every fort-night att the
furthest standing upon the end, so you may keepe them good a year 2 or 3, then
when you list you may distill them in a Limbeck, you may take for every London
pck a gallon of rosewater, and that which coms first is the best.

To make Treakle Water.

Take an ounce of the shavings of harts-horne; boile it in 3 pintes of
Carduus water till it coms to a quarte, then take the roots of Elicampana and Gentium, Cyprus and Tormentill of each one ounce, carduus, angelia and cytrons
rinds of each an ounce, Burrage's flowers, bugloses flowers, rosmary, flowers
marygoes flowers of each 2 ounces; then take a pound of the best old treakle,
dissolve it in 6 pintes of white wine and 3 pintes of read roses water and infuse
all together in an earthen vessell exceeding close stopped 24 houres, and then
still it in a glasse still, or an ordinary rose water still that is very close) and
still it with a slow fire, and as long as there is any strength in the water,
then mix it all the water together, and sweeten it with sugar according to your
liking, Take 4 spoonfull of it att a time and so less according the age of the party
to a new-born child but a small spoonfull att a time.
It is good against any infection and in the small pox and measels, it gently
brings them forth, it is good in a feaver and if the heat be Violent, then add
Some Syrup of cytrons, so much as will give it a quickness. It is good to be
taken in the yellow yander, and if it be taken before the fitts, it doth in a
Short time helpe the ague. It helpes the wormes in children if taken att night
when they goes to bed, and to new born children it is good for winds and griping
paines.

To make Sweet water.

Take a Botle of rose-water and putt it into a still and draw out 3 pintes, then
putt in the 3 pintes again and draw out 2 pintes; then putt to it 2 grains of
Civett 4 of musk and 8 of amber-greece; So putt all into a glasse very well stopped,
and putt it in a thing of hott water and there lett it stand all day; then take it out, and when it is cold straine it and keepe it to your use very close.

To dry and parfume Roses.

Take the best leaves of Roses, then putt cleane white paper within the still,
then straw all over the still a line of roses and then some of the pouder, (under
prescribed) and then more roses and then pouder and so till the still be allmost full,
then cutt a paper round to be just within the still, and putt noe other cover upon it,
but keepe a good heate in the still; and as the cakes dryed turne the other side
& when

A Good Water Broth for the Face.

Take a quarte of Snailes, wash them in salt, & water them in 2 faire

a pinte of butter-milk, of Virgins wax & turpantine an ounce of each,

Another Sweet Water.

Honey water for the Haire.

To Keepe the teeth cleane & white.

Take lemons & distill them, & so washe your teeth with the

To make Paste for Hands.
Take halfe a pod of figgs, as many reasins of the Sun stoned, halfe a
pod of bitter Almonds blanched; Beate all these Severally in a mortar Very Small,
mix them together with 2 lemons, the seeds taken out & the lemons minced,
2 hand full of beans meale; Boile all these together in a pinte of white wine
Venigar stirring them continually, & when it has boiled that it sticks noe
more to the pipkin it was boiled in, take it off & putt it in a gally-pott
for your vse.
To make Lip's Salue.
Take half a pinte of rose-water, putt in about the bigness of a large
walnut of fresh butter without any salt, and about the bigness of a large
grey pea of Bees-wax & the bigness of a walnut of fine loafe sugar Boile
all together, & when cold take the Salue of the water.
A Very good Washe for the Face to take out heatt &
make the skin cleare.
Take as much white Sugar Candy as will laye vpon a sheling, the Same
quantity of allum, beate the white of a new lead egg Very well, & then putt
in the allum & sugar candy & beate them all-together, then putt them into
a pinte of white wine & one ounce of oyle of tartar, one ounce of oyle of
Almonds & shake them all-together for 2 houres without staning still, then
putt in a penny-worth of Camphir tyed vp in a bag, & lett it hange in the
botle, Shake it when you vse it.
To Washe the face. Lady Foster.
Take black Snailes & pull of the black strings out of the back, then
wash them & lay them in a cloth, & so still them.
A Sweet water to burne vpon a fire-pan a litle att a time
Take 2 ounces of Beniamin, & as much of Storax, a quarter of an ounce of
Cloves, all these beate severall into pouder, 6 grains of musck & as much of
the Spiritt of roses; grind the musk to pouder & 6 grains of Civett; you must
take a Spoonfull or two of rose-water to melt the Civett; take a quarte of
Damas-rose-water & putt it in a glasse, & then take all these things & putt
into it; then shake your glasse 2 or 3 times in a day, & when the glasse is allmost
out, you may fill it again to the Sweets, & it will make the water sweet 2 or
3 years, and so vse it as you please
To dry & parfume Roses.

Take the best leaves of roses. &c. look in Seilling page 90.
For readness or pushes in the Face
Take the iuice of white grapes, before they be ripe, being Suddenly press out 1 pinte
of the depirated iuice of housleeck, 2 pintes of the purest iuice of lemons 12 ounces
Sulphur Vinim Very finely pouderd & seached 2 ounces, of roach allum half an ounce
Storax 3 dragms, White sugar Candy 6 dragms, Camphir 1 dragme; lett all these be
putt together in a bouth-head & buried in horses-dung the Space of eight days,
Afterward lett the liquor be filtred thorough browne paper, Afterward add a pinte
of the purest rectified spiritt of wine. With this you are to dabb your face with a linnen
cloth att night, the next morning wash it of with the water of white lillies distilled.

To dry Roses . another way

Take the budds & cutt them from the whites then stich a clove in the
midst of them & putt them into a preserving glass, and lay a lay of
Roses & a lay of orris powder beniamin storax & a little amber-greece. SO
lay them close stopping the glasse, & sett it on the sun, when it has stood
five days open the glasse & stew them & sett them on the Sun again
till they be thorow dry.

To dry Rose for linnen . another way

Putt your Roses as you use into an ordinary still butt not on the top
of your still at all, make but little fire; when they grow damp putt Some
sweet powder on them, keep them stirring till they be dry. Sow them in
baggs for linnen.

To washe the Face with.

Make a posset with milk luke warme brandy sack & a little juice of lemon
take of the curdle cleare, then blanche & beate fine some cocombers seeds, putt
a little sack to them as you beate them, putt them into your possett, sett it
to warme gently over the fire, then sett it by for 2 days. Warme it again
So straine it out.

Another good water to wash t Face with.

Take the second water that pearle-barley was boiled in & blanche some
bitter allmonds in it & beate them well & make allmonds milk with your
barly-water, and if you have a rough read flashing putt in a little -
salt-peter.

Another water for the Face

Take the distilled water of the sperme of froggs half a pinte, with an ounce
of white poppies seeds, make an emulsion thereof like allmonds milk, straine
it and add therto of mercury sublimat 5 grains & 6 drops of the iuice
of lemons & keepe it for your use.
Att your going to bed you must dipe a linnen cloth in it & wash yr face
and wash it of in the morning with milk.

For Pimples in the face.

Take the white of a new lead egg & 2 spoonfull of oyle of Violetts of read
-roses water & iuice of sorrell of each a spoonfull, of flower of sulphur half -
a dragme and half a spoonfull of white wine venigar mix all these things
together and make an Unguent thereof for your use
To Smoothe the Skin and take away Freckles
Take Oyle of sweet almonds an ounce, new draw Oyle of tartar half an ouce
putt them & shake them together & it will become white. Wett the skin there-
-with att night when you goes to bed.


111

A Pomatum

A caull of Veale laid in soack in water shifting it two days twice a
day then ring it out with your hands & beate it well in a mortar, put to it
a little white wine, putt it in a gally-pott, tye it close & boile it in a Skellet
of water & boile it 2 or 3 houres, then straine it out & worke it well with
a spoon in an earthen Basom, then take a little white sugar candy, fine
beaten & scarified, then mix it well to a Body.

Another Pomatum

Take a pod of the fatt of a young hogg, skin it & cutt it grosly lett it
laye in water 2 days shifting it once a day: then take a handfull of the
roots of Sollomans, wash & scrape them cleane, beate them with a pennyworth
of cocombers seeds in a stone mortar; then putt your suet in & beate all
together, putt it in a gally-pott close coured & sett it in a skellet of water to
melt for 2 houres; then poure it into a Basom; Beate it cold. the next day
putt it again into the pott to melt in the skellet as above with a quarter
of a pinte of rosewater, so lett it stand a melting for 2 houres in boiling
water, then straine it out into a Basom; when it is cold, beate it up &
pott it.

A Paste to scowre hands

Take half a pod of peache's curnels, scald them in hott water & blanche
them in cold water & stamp them in a stone mortar, and when they be
well beaten, take the whites of 4 eggs, stirr them well together, putt to it
a little white wine & the iuice of a Lemon & a litle white rose water,
mingle all these in a mortar, then sett it on the fire in an earthen pott,
till it beginne to be a litle hard, then take it of & keepe it in a gally-
pott close covred; and when you use it, take as much as a nuttmegg
& rub it all over your hands & wrists with a litle bean's flower & water,
& lett it laye till it is dry not wiping them, yet you may dap m on a
dry cloth. This will make your hands white, smooth soft & moist.

To make Camphir Balls

First take fowre Allmonds faire scared, knocke those in a mortar
reasonably well, one ounce of Camphir putt unto e Allmonds & knock m
a good while together with a spoonfull or two of rosewater, then putt in
a litle wheat flower; then take half a pod of Cassell-soape, shave it & putt
it into the rest of the compounds & beate them all well together in a
mortar, and after the putting of the soape, putt in a litle flower & a litle
rose-water; all these compounds being well beaten together, then take it
out and worke it into balls & putt them into a box close to dry.

To make Boards looke well
Scowre them twice or thrice in a yeare with fullers earth & sand
& once a weeke or when they are spotted rub them with a long brushe
& sweepe them cleane. Thus they will looke well.
Mrs Huxly after they are scowred with fullers earth & sand take some tur-
merick, bruise it & boile it in water, then with a cleane cloth wash over the
boards, & when it is dry rub the Roome.
82





To cleare Sylver plates to look as new.
Firest neall your plates in the fire, which is putting the plates
in a hott fire till it be as bright as a coale, when it is read hott, pull
it out & set it on the hearth, & when it is cold putt it into a boiling
pott of water, be sure it boiles when you putt it in, then take a dung
called argoll (which you may have at a Drugster) & salt. Lett the salt
be more in proprotion then the argoll. Be sure not to stirr it with
any horn or brafs for it will burne it black, but with a stick.
Now you take 2 ounces of salt to one ounce & a half of argoll,
& this proportion to 2 or 3 quartes of spring water, so when tis
white it is enough. Take it out with a stick & be sure you Keepe
your water continually boiling till you take it out.

To dye Straw read, marry or yellow.
Putt the straws into faire water & putt to them as much roach allum
as will make the water of a blakish colour, lett them boile halfe an
houre, them take them out, & put some of them into a thing by them
- selves, & putt faire water to them, If it be a thing [that] will hold 2 quarts
you may putt in half a nod of brazill & a dragme of cochenill a little
bruised & putt into a ragg; lett this boile till it be read, then take them
out, & putt into the same liquor more straws, and it need be more water,
& a quarter of a nod of logwood, lett this boile for a murry colour; To
make a yellow you must boile them straws in faire water & a quarter
of a nod of turmerick & a little safran, soe lett in boile to a yellow.

To dye Straws Blew.
Take chamberly att least 7 or 8 days old, (for the older it is the better)
poure it from the grounds & putt it into a cleane carthen hott, then take
an ounce of roach-allum beaten into pouder & putt it into [the] chamberlye
then take an ounce of the best Spanish Indigo blew & pouder it very well
(which must be thus done) wett your mortar first with a litle chamberlye,
warme, then putt in your Indigo, and as you beat it, putt in a little cham-
berly it will make it beat [the] better, & take of all the great hollow which
lye uppermost, & lett it be as small as flower. Then warme your Lye by
the fire not very hott and putt in your Indigo and in a day & a night
you may use it, stirr it often; Soake your straws first in a little cham-
berly and allum mixt together, but except your straws be very white it
will not take a blew. Wee dye green in the same liquor that wee doe
Blew, only you must dye it yellow first.

A Varnish for severall Colours.
Take gum-lake & gum-sandrak of each a litle quantity, & mastick
a fifth part part of the quantity of the other, & putt them all together in
a glaSse of pure rectifyed spirit of wine close stopped, & lett them stand
2 or 3 days, and in that time the gums diSsolve, or to hasten the diSso
-lution you may sett the glaSse in warme water, see [that] [the] glaSse be close
stopt & not washed; putt so much spirit of wine as may cover [the] gums
& mch, if it be to thick putt in more Spirit of wine, if to thin more
gums. And when you would Varnish any thing, you must make it as
hott as the sun would make it in the heat of Summer, els it will not
take the Varnish, and doe it 4 or 5 times over with a fine haire brush
vpon tin it will looke like gold.

For Black.
Take lampblack (calcined Ivory is the best) putt it in a dish &
add the above Varnish to it.

For Greene.
Take orpin & ground & blew Verditer of each equall parts, & putt the
Varnish to the pouders in a glaSse, but dry the pouders well first.

For Blew.
Take Blew Verditour very dry with the above mentioned varnish.

For Read.
take pure sanguis Dracsnis in pouder with the above Varnish.

For a perfect Clear.
Take Gum-lake alone.

For an Ordinary Varnish to use with colours.
The Shell or drop-lake disSolved as above. If any impurity be in it,
you may straine it.
You may perfume it by diSsolving amber-greene in it.

For a Varnish to clear white paper for windows.
Take half a pod of the best & whitest strasberries turpantine warme
it in a skellet, then putt to it one ounce & a half of mastick beaten to
pouder, stirring it over a soft fire till it be diSsolved; then putt in half
a pod of turpentine oyle, mixing it with a spoon over the fire. The
paper you cleare must be done over & wett with allums water, & lett
dry again before the varnish be applyed. It must be laid on with
a graving toole.

For broken China.
Burnt allum will Ciment China.





To parfume Gloves.
Take white gloves dipe them in fontaine water, and
waing them & dry them with a stick for that purpose,

composition. For six paires. Take half an ounce -
of benjamin and a quarter of an ounce of storax;

much Calamus aromaticus, and grande all those upon
a marble with rose-water, which you must after putt
into a vessel; adding thereunto the 16th part of a
pinte of rose water; so put in the gloves and to -
vestirr it 3 times; it will be the better if you add
a little muske and grinde it with the rest; add also
the bigness of a nutt of gum-dragon strrped in rose-
-water, and so putt alltogether upon the gloves.

Receits for Surgery  & phisick
To make the stone called Lapis Prunella
Take a Creusiple putt into it two pounds of Saltpeter So -
lett it in the midle of a Chearcole fire, (the Coles must be
putt upon every side almost as high as the brim of the pott)
you must blow it continually to make the fire the hotter,
till your Saltpeter be all melted and boyles, then when it
boyles have ready half an ounce of brimstone and as much
of the Roth allum, Brake the brimstone & allum into three
equal parts, then while it boyles through in one Lump of
Brimstone which will burne and tam the saltpeter, a little
after it hath done burning throw in a Lump of alum
which will smoke and hiss a good while and a litle after
it has done hissing throw in another lump of brimstone,
So observing the same order throw in the six lumps; then
lett it boyle a quarter of an houre after all is in; then
with a strong pair of tongs take it of the fire for feare
with to suddin cooling it will  breake the pot, then have
ready as many brass potts & ladles, then poure into them
so much of the liquor as will make a Cake half inche
thick, so lett them stand til they be hard, which will be
in a quarter of an houre; then take them out & keep
them for use. the use of it is thi.
It is most Excellt good for a sore throat, it is good for
a feaver and it will cure a pleuresy with out letting blood

the manner of taking of it

You must scrape as much as will lay upon a grate and putt
it into 3 or 4 spoonfull of beer, then take 3 or 4
of the frop of beer in the mouth and lett it distill lesurely
in the mouth. Soe take it once an houre for feaver or pluresy
you must take more for a pleuresy then for a sore throat.
It is good for sore Eyes if you take it in a quill & blow it into e Eyes. 20 grains is good for the Stone to be taken in white wine
or beare which you please.

Oyntment good for payne in the back or
Stomach, to help digestion or payne in the heart
or any Cold or Cough or anoint the temples.
Take a quarte of the best salett Oyle or so much of -
the Oyle of roses, & putt it into a long gally-pott, to it
wilde time. read Mint. wormwood. hysopp. Balme of each -
two  handfull, of read roses leaves ginger one ounce. half
an ounce of mace grossly beaten, chopp, the hearbs a little -
mingle the spices; have an ounce of bay berries; put all -

pott, close it up with paste, sett it in a stillet of water,
keep the water as high as the Oyle coms, lett it stand -

Wine against a Consumption.
Take 6 quartes of the best white wine, putt it in an Earthen
pitcher Stopp it close with a Corke & putt it into a Kettle of -
Water and lett it stand so long in the boyling water till the
wine boyles, then putt into it 2 ounces & half of succory
roots sliced, & lett it boyle halfe a quarter of an houre, then
take the pitcher out of the water, then putt into it the
rines of 2 lemons & 1 orange paring it very thin, with
half an ounce of Cinamon & 6 ounces of sugar.
After these thing are all putt in together, the Pitcher is to
be covered up close for 24 hours, after that you must -
shake it well together, then straine it through a flanel
Strainer & botle it up for use.

Dyett drink to clean Blood of Mr. Dobins.
Take 6 Oranges. 6 lemons. 2 lb of raisins Sun.
Juice of garden Scurvygrass. Bookline. Watercress of -
each a quarte. Elderbuds six good handfull, horse =
-radish half a pound. the lemons & Oranges must be
thin Sliced Rind & all. these are for twelve Gallons.
Remedy for the yellow Jandse
A pinte of White wine, half a pinte of Celendine
water, as much dragons water, three penny worth of Saffran
two of turmerick.  Infuse all these together till half
of it be wasted & then drink it att 3 mornings.
A Medecine against the plage.  Small pox. Mesels.
and divers diseases & surfeits composed by Dr. Butler.
Take 3 pints of Malmsey or Musiadine, and boyle one handfull
of hearbs of grace and one handfull of sage in it, until a third
part be wasted; then take that of the fire, straine it & putt
thereto one penneworth of long pepper; half an ounce of ginger,
a quarter of an ounce of nuttmeg being first well beaten,
then sett that on the fire again, and lett that boyle a little
longer; then take it of the fire again & putt therein 4 penny
- worth of mithridate 2 penny worth of teriacle of Gennes
a quarter of a pinte of strong angelico water or of the best
aquavita you can gett. Tye this as you love your life.
Take it alwayes warme both morning & evening a Spoonfull
or two if you are infected and sweatt a litle thereupon; If
you Be not infected then take half a spoonfull both morning
& Evening during the time of infection, Under God trust
to this for there was never Man, Woman or Child ever
to this yet that were deceived, if the heart were not -
mortifyed before this drink came. Probatum est.

Physick & Surgery

How to make Gascoins powder.

Take white amber. Read corall. Orient pearles. Harts horne
crabs eyes of each one ounce. of East Bezar one dragme
the tops of the claws of a Crabb (so far as is black) as much -
in weight as all the rest. Beate them severally into powder
then search them through a fine search then with Jelly -
of hawthorne the spirit of sack of a little Safran make it
into little round balls and dry them with a moderate heat.
This powder is Very good to take away all ague to be given
when the cold fitt is coming in a little ale prophet drinke, & to
be used 3 severall time, and to be given in a feaver to drive -
away any thing from the heart or the stomach & to cause sleep
it must be given at night when you goe to bed.
It is good to prevent the small pox if taken 3 or 4 times. It is very good
to kill the worms in Children you must give to a child -
5 or 6 grains at one time to men or women 10 or 13 grains
In my Ladys Kent prouder Hey putt in a little -
Contraurva and you may putt in a little Cochenelle.

For a Feaver.

Take a penny worth of blew corrints and one penny worth
of mirridale, pound them in a stone mortar till they are -
like a salve; then take a sowre sinell orange & cutt it throw
the midle and spread the salve upon the juicey ride of e.
Orange & bind one halfe to the one wrist & the other half
to the other an houre before the fitt coms when the orange
and Salve growe dry renew it with fresh. My Lady Purbex.

A Medecine for the Ague.

Take Onions. Sage & saffran beaten together till they
com to a sort of a poulles and aply it to the wrists and
Sole of the feet and drink a kind of Safran thea that -
day the fitt is expected.



124

Physick & Surgery.

For the Bite of a Mad Dog.

Take two quartes of strong Ale, one pennyworth of triacle
two spoonfull of scraped pewter. One handfull of rue;
a head of garlick. Boyle all these together till it is but
a quarte; Give it att 3 mornings first letting blood and
eating some fo the dogs liver if it to be had.

To Make the Black Plaster.

Take 3 quarters & better of Virgin Wax, and a pinte of
the best Salett Oyle, melt this together in a large brass pan
then take halfe a pound of the best white lead, grind it on
a painters stone Very fine, then take the Oyle from the
fire, lett it coole a litle, then putt in the lead stirring it
together sett it on again lett it boyle half an houre take
it of the fire. putt in 2 ounces of Mastick 2 of Olibanum
2 ounces of myrr. 2 of frankinsence. 2 of Bergame pitch
2 of barkamahat; Lett them be all finely poudred & searcht.
Putt this pan into another pan for fear of running over.
then putt in all the gums, stirring it till it has done rising
then sett it on the fire again, lett it boyle half an houre
stirring it still, then take a quarter of an ounce of Camphir
finely beaten to pouder, strew it into the pan, stirring it
all the while; So lett it boyle till it be black, then take
it of, stirring it till it be cold; then oyle your hand,
and the Board & soe make it into Rolles, this keepe
choice as your life. this is that which cure the
toothach or head ach.

The Bitter Drink.

Take 2 handfull of Carduus, the like of wormwood & the
same of mint, putt these mixed together into a cold still, and poure
on them a gallon of Brandy; then distill of keeping a slow fire,
the first running will be very strong, soe that you may mix
it with the last as you please.

Physick & Surgery.

Another Bitter Drinke.

Take of the tops of the lesser Centaury and Camamill flowers
of each as much as you may well take up between your two fore-
fingers and thumb; of the roote of Gention the bigness of a-
nuttmeg; of the leaves of Senna and Seed of carduus of each
a dragme. Boyle these ingredients gently in half a pinte of -
spring water to the consumption of a third part. Straine the liquor
and drinke it in the morning every sixth day, & then omit the
use of the wine.
Of the root of Elecampana a litle bruised one ounce. Roman-
wormwood and rue each a small handfull. Filing of Steele-
one ounce. Cinamon 2 dragmes; Infuse them in 3 pints of-
Rhenish Wine, and after two days drink a small sack glasse-
three times each day.
When the Wine is drunk putt half the quantity of every-
things, (but the steel is for the same ingredients) with fresh-
wine and drinke as before.
It is good for the payne in the stomach & green sickness-
being the purge & bitter drink of D.r Nicolson.

D.r Lowers Drink for a Consumption

Take of sarsaparilla 3 ounces. China 2 ounces. Yellow Sander
half an ounce. Shavings of hartshorne and Ivory of each 3 dragms
Infuse and boyle these in 4 quarts of running Water gently, till
a pint or more be wested. then add of maidenhaire, speed-well-
Ground Ivy scabious. Coltsfoot of each one handfull. Raisins of the
Sun stoned one handfull, boyle them till the fourth part be wasted-
adding att the latter part of boyling half an ounce of liquorish
two spoonfull of Honey, a quarter of a pinte white wine. Boyle
it till there be noe scum left then strain it and lett it sette and
powre of the cleare, and soe Keep it in a glass bottle, and drink of
it two or three times a day

For Spiting Blood.

Dragons blood and salt prunell and plantin's water.

D.r Lower's Tincture.

Take of Giaxum the smallest chips you can gett; leaves of
Senna, Liquorish, anyseed, Coriander seed, Elicampana roots,
dryed of each 2 ounces; Raisins stoned one pound; 2 ounces
of Garroway seed, a quarter of an ounce of jalap. steep these
in 3 quarts of the best aqua vita 8 or 10 days, then poure
all of, and take 3-4 or 5 sponfull in any ilness in the stomach
at any surfeit or fitts of the stone in the Kidneys'

My Lady Danbys Rec.s for a Sore Breast.

Take the cornes of the fore leggs of a stone horse, wash
them in white wine; dry them in an oven, then wash-
them again, and when dry beate them fine enough to sift-
then take 20 grains with the same quantity of mace (pou-
dred also) in sack or any other liquor. take night & morning
fast 2 houres after.
Be sure to purge once a fortnight (all the while you-
take this pouder) with manna or some other gentle thing.

For the Migraine in the Head

Take Sentry Bettony and steep it in white wine, and-
when you think it strong enough of the hearbs as you may-
Know by the bitternes of it, straine it & keep it in a great
mouth glass, or steepe it in a wide mouth'd glass & drink
a good glass of it every morning.

Another for the same purpose.

Take half an ounce of male pionys roots, when they be-

Candy finely beaten; and take as much of these as will lye
upon six pence, in a morning fasting, mingle it in a spoon-

of Bear after it.

The Wound Drink good for any Inward-
Bruise. Impostume stopping in the breath,
Strangury, fistula or any outward sore.
Gather the following hearbs in Aprill or May, and if you
have not present use of the drink, dry them in a Roome, where
neither Sun nor Wind may com to them, turne them once a day
and when they be thorow dry, mingle them all together, of each
a like proportion, & put them up in papers baggs, so keepe
them at your pleasure all the year, those that are buds, -

To heale or cleane a wound & Keep it from
proud flesh.

First washe it with butter & bear, and then spread Basilion
vpon Lint, and lye it vpon the Soar; If the wound be Very ill, wash
it and dress it twice a day, when it begins to heale, once a day
will serve.

To Skin any Bruise where the skin is rubbed of.

Take fresh butter and Bees wax, melt them together and dipp fine-
Linning rags in it in the manner of a cearcloth.

To cure any cutt or wound when it is first made.

A Receit to make Read pouder.

Take Scabius. Pimpernell. Bethony. Cardus. Tormentill roots
& all. Dragon. Sage. Angelica. Divills bit. Rue. Scordium.
Winter Cherrys. Egrimony. Pellitory of Spaine. Pellitory of
the Wall Palma Crista. Horehound. Vervane. Wormwood. Endive
Savory. Fetherfew. Remitory. Balme. Burrage. the tops of unsett
hysop. the tops of Rosmary. Bithony. Celendine. Hartshorne
with all other cordial hearbs you can think of, of each an
handfull. Then take of Elicampana roots. liquorish. Dragon's
roots and all, Angelico roots of each two ounces, scrape your
roots clean & slice them all; Shred your hearbs small & putt
them to steep in an earthen pott, with as much white wine
as will cover them, which will be about 6. quarts; So lett
them stand 2 or 3 days, then take a pound of the purest
bole Armoniack made exceeding fine, and temper soe much of
this liquor with it as will make it like pappe, Sett it in the
sun to dry, and as it drye putt more liquor to it, Doe so till
all the liquor is dryed vp to half a pinte; which keepe to
mingle with these cordials, Take 4 ounces of Mithridate, 4 onces
of Diascordium. 4 ounces of prepared hartshorne 2 ounces of
Alchermes; one dragme of saffran, mingle them well with
the liquor & so putt it to the rest & lett dry in the sun, till
it will make vp in Cakes, and then keep them very dry.
It is Sovrain for all distempers, and to be given with cold
distilled Dragon or Angelico or a litle Sack as much as will lay
on a groate. they must be kept in a breathing sweat one
houre, and tehn drink some posset made with hartshorne &&
Marygolds. the experience of it has been very great.

An Excellent sulep or safran water & milk water
To a quarte of whey a small handfull of Damask rose leaves
from buds with a small handfull of femirary infused in clary-
-fyed whey halfe an hour close stopt. a litle boyling. Take the
milk curds of the whey & then straine it. Drink it coole.
For the milk water Lute the still very close and a soft fire
let it stand till its cold before you straine it.
For the safran water or cooling julels leaving out the safran
4 pounds of pearle barley make it into a Gallon of barley water
3 pounds of saxsafras 3 pounds arrice roots shied thin one pound
of Cardamons bruised one handfull of unsett bysop one handfull
of Coltsfoot 2 handfull of mowseare 2 handfull of Dendelyon
infuse this in a piphin & paste it up close sett it on a gentle
fire 48 hours lett it stand till it is cold then strayne it and add
to it 18 pence of safran for feavers or any such distemper excellent
good for a consumption take one third of a quarte morning & night
If you want of sleepe sweeten it with a syrup of cowshys. If faint
with Jellyflowers. if gripe a handfull of read roses infused and
some sugar. For a consumption take it 2 months dayly in bed
att 5 in the morning one third of a quarte and another going to bed.
without safran it keepe but 7 days with it 14 days.

For an Ague cake or Bigg-belly or worms in children

Take unsett leekes 2 handfull wormswood 2 handfull. fetherfew 2
handfull, chope them small as you doe pott hearbs, then fry them in
a frying pan or large fireshovell and wett them with an Oxe gall
doe not burn them nor frye them Crispe & drye but like a Tansey
when they are soe fryed that they are all soft & well soaked in the gall
then putt it into a linning bagg & lay it as warme as the child can -
well sufer it all over the child's belly from above the pitt of the stomach
before the navell. Lay it on att night when the child goes to Bed &
bind it on with the blanket or a roller and when it coms of, have
a peece of flanell ready to putt upon the Childs Belly & stomach in
the day time, for these things att night will make the child tender in
the day

the day, and apt to take cold, this bagg must be layd on 3 nights
together, & 2 or 3 nights before the full of the moone will do best.
the same hearbes will serve all the 3 nights, only they must be
wett every night with fresh gall, and if they be grou'n more
dry att last then att first as they will be lett them soake a
little in the gall before you frye them. In case you cannot
gett green wormwood or fetherfew, dryed will serve. my Lady Purbex.

A Receit for an Ague

Take two ounces of plague water cold distilled & 24 grains of
salt wormwood. So mix it and give it to the patient an houre
before the fitt. Repeated if needs require.

Another Receit for an Ague

take a whole nuttmegg the like quantity of Roach alum,
soe pound it in a Mortar & steepe it in a pinte of white wine
for a night, then divide it in two parts, & give half of it to
the patient half an houre before the fitt, & the other part, half
an houre after the fitt.

For the migrain in the Head

Take Centry and Bittony & steep it in white wine, and when
you think it strong enough of the hearbs, as you may know by
the bitterness of it, straine it and keepe it in a great wide mouths
glass, or steep it in a wide mouth glass. Drink a good glass of it
every morning fasting.

Another for the migrain in the Head or convulsions fitts

Take Conserve of read piane flowers, to be take night or morning,
or more. Aqua Epileptcia take in fitts or when feumes doth
arise a spoonfull or more att a time. Item for the migrain in the
head or any giddnes take half an ounce of male peoney's roots when
they be scraped and dryed in an oven & half an ounce of male peony berries
half an ounce of nuttmegs. a quartet of an ounce of Bittony. a quarter
of an ounce of Rosemary tops, both being dryed. Beate all these into fine
pouder then mingle them very well together with a quarter of a pound
of white sugar Candy finely beaten.

For the Iaundice.
Take 2 Oranges, cutt a piece out of the top as bigg as a halfe
Crowne, take out most of the meate, & putt in 4 penny worth of
Safran into each Orange, putt on the piece that was first cutt of,
wrap them in a wett paper & putt them over White Ashes vnder the
Embers of a woode fire; the next morning wipe them clean, & beate
them in a mortar, & putt them (with 2 handfulls of the Iuice of
Celendine bruised) into a Botle of Rhenish; Shake it for 3 dayes
then lett it stand 12 or 24 houres, and drink half a pinte att a time
Morning, Afternoone & going to Bed.
Poure it of carefully as you vse it and it will be cleare to the last
& that if it setle will be soe too.
If it be to Sharpe, putt what Syrup you please (as you drink it) in
each draught but not in the whole botle. syrup of Althea two aunces
mingled with one ounce of Syrup of Violetts.
The rest of an Orange putt in Brandy & burn't with a nuttmegg
cutt in thin slices (to gett out the oyle) & sweetned & drunck hott,
gives ease in the Colick. the like does seeds boyles in beer or Ale
and when straind drunk hott.
Master Sherrard's Sear cloth.
Take one pound of read lead poudred, one quarte of Salad Oyle,
& when you have well mingled them together, sett them on a gentle
fire constantly stirring them with a stick, and lett it not boyle to
fast, when you see it look browne dip in the cloth & roule them up,
they will turne pale when they are cold, & the longer they are kept
the better. It will cure all bruises, aches & swellings that a sear cloth
is applyed to, but most excellent for Sore Breast. If you dip in your
cloths iust when you see it turne borwne, then they will be dry
in an instant, & when it is sonnest dry then it is rightest done
you must rub them when they are dry with a Sleeke Stone or the botton of a quarte botle.
To cure a Speck in the Eye
Take a litle Selendine & pound it with a litle small beer, straine
it, and drop it into your Eye night & morning.

For a Feaver.
Take a Gallon of white wine or Claret wne (that which is
most delicious more then strong) putt into it an ounce & halfe
of Iesuist pouder finely poudred, half a handfull of litle
Centrie flowers, two dragmes of salt of the same plant, and as
much of good whie tartar, or in leu of these 2 salts) 2 dragms
of salt Armoniac Very Well purifyed, one dragme or 2 of sas-
-fras wood cutt thin; or one dragme of Ienipers berries, or any
other Aromaticks; Lett all be infused in for 24 hours on
warme Embers; putt it in a well closed Vessell; then straine it
& keep it for your vse. It is an Infusion for a Feaver.
and it may be given without the pouder before the fitts.
The doze of it may be taken 4 ounces att a time to be taken
twice a day att least in a morning fasting 2 hours before
meat or 3 hours after going to bed, or att all those times taking the vsual doze of the Iesuist pouder in a glass of sack or
white wine or Rhenish wine, one houre or two before each fitt
for 3 or 4 fitts, the body being duely prepared by Vomiting
or both as the Physitian shall advise.
To make Ancusa or the read Tincture
Take 2 pounds of the best fresh butter unwashed, butt extreamely well
beaten from butermilk, and the next day after churming, sett it
on a soft heat of Embers to melt, lett it not boile in the least,
then poure of the butter from the milky-bottom, and scum what
dross ariseth on the top; and to every pound of butter thus prepared,
take six ounces of the roots of Alkanet or wild Buglos, (for it is
Known by boh names to the physical Hearbsweomen) May is the
best Month to make it in) the roots that are read on the outside
which grow in Marlyt ground & rarely any where else; wipe
gently the dust of them, but doe not scrape or washe them, for
that will take of the colour; then slice of the outward part of
the roots or stripe it from the pith, for if they be young (which
are the best) they will stripe clean from the pith) and cutt it in
peeces about an intche long, but none of the pitch. So putt it into
Your butter, then sett it as before on a soft heat, and lett it never
boile. keepe it coverd, and take of the scumme that riseth. When the
colour is very Orient and pleaseth you, & that it tasted. well of the
roots

The Vertues for Coughs & Consumption

It is good for inward bruises or broken Veines or spitting of blood
or decay of lungs, Impostumes or Ulceres or Stone, giving the quantity
of a large Wallnut (night and morning for nine days) att a time,
and then omitt it, and take it again where any setled dissease is, and
where heat or dislike to sack is not, It is best to give it in that, -
unless in case of the stone, there white wine is best; Make the liquor
to give it in so hott that it may dissolve it without heat of e fire,
for that makes it rank. It is good for burns or scalds, with flaxes
or fellons att the beginning on a burn; when you have well anointed
it, lay a peece of fine paper well spread with it, for white flaxes -
or fellons mix it with crums of bread & lay on. If any one have a -
bruise in the legg drop some of it.
If you cannot gett the read rootes which are not so common; the
white will make good, the other is better, for you may have 1000 -
white for 1 read, vnless you light in a place it delights.

Lozanges for the head & Stomach to help Wind & ill Digestion.

Take a pound of the best loafe sugar and boile it vp to a candy height with
a dozen spoonfull of Damas-rose-water, then take it of e fire, and putt to it,
these pouders following; One nuttmegg; six times as much Cinamon, & six -
times as much liquorish; nine cloves; 3 leaves of mace; Gallingall as much -
as half a nuttmegg; Safram, musk and Ambregris of each 3 grains; pouder
these fine & search them, Stirr them into the sugar; So poure it on a paper
and when it is cold cutt it into Lozanges.

My Lord Killmorrey's Dyet Drink

Take of the Inner Barke fo the roots of a young Ash cutt into long
slices of the breath and lenght of ones finger, Six handfull of the
green rinds of Ivy, 4 handfull of the yellow roots of Dockes pick't
and bruised, as much of the rootes of polipodie of the Oake newly ga=
thered cleansed & bruised; of the Kernels of the Keyes of Ash picket out
of their Huskes 3 handfull, of Britony, Balme Hartes eare fine pouder
of each two handfull, of Rosemary one handfull; Boile all these very
tender in as much water as will make 8 gallons of warte, putt in
a quantity of malt according to the strenght you would have it, In
your worte boile some hops and 5 handfull of Egrimony and 4 of -
sage; Run the worte from the hearbs and hops, and then run it -
thorow a Jelly-Bagg leasurely that it may be cleane; Barme it, cleanse
it & run it vp into a convenient Vessell.
this is to be drunk att 10 or 12 days old.

To  make Ale good against the Stone.

Take 3 gallons of the best worte, then take a handfull of saxifrax, a -
handfull of peestone, the Kernell of a handfull of Ash-Keyes, A handfull &
a half of Eringo's roots vnpreserved and a handfull of parsly; dry all these
well & beate them in a mortar, and boile them in worte one houre, and
more, Then sett it a working as you doe other Ale, and so sun it vp, -
and drink it att 3 or 4 daies old, you may putt a few hops in it to keep -
it from sowring; You must drink a good draught in the morning fasting
with nuttmeggs & sugar, if you please, and again att 4 a clock in the after-
-noone, walking or vsing some moderate exercise with it.

A Dyett Drink for the Spleen & Liver.

You must take wild sage, fenell fementory and Carduus benedictus of each -
a handfull, and boile them in 3 gallons of ale, and when it has done working, you
may add 3 ounces of salsaparilla, 4 ounces of senna, 2 ounces of polipodis, 1 ounce
of sasafras, 1 ounce of Jalap, 1 ounce & half of liquorish 3 of fennell rootes, 1 ounce
of anyseed, pound them all & hang them in a fine linnen bagg in yr Barrel of Ale
Bruising them with your hands in the Ale, and take half a pinte of e Juice of
brooke Lyme, and as much of the Juice of scurvy-grass, and putt them also in your
Barrell. This I think to be the fittest meane to repaire your natural heath and
to strengthen the Liver and Spleene.

For the Spleen.

Take of Ash Keyes of the Inner Barke of Ash a small quantity, a quarter of an handfull of Bur-
-rage flowers, the like proportion of Elder's flowers of Inner Barke (in the winter's time) you -
may take Elder's rootes. Boile these in the third part of a pinte of sack, sweeten it with sugar
and drink it when the fitts coms. Boile your sack till the fourth part be consumed.

For the Beating of the Heart.

Take pills 3 att a time a quarter of an houre before diner, att any time
when you find your stomach full, or any thing that offend e stomach. If
your heart beates commonly take 2 or 3 spoonfull of cordial liquor, and some
 time you may take Aple-water, Albermy or Burrage water or a little Claret
Wine burnt. Then boile in e wine tops of burrage, of balme of lemons
thyme & a litle nuttmegg and fine loafe sugar; and in a quarter of a pinte
burne these, mix a litle Albermy before you take it. but take e pills twice
in a weeke before.

To make Salt of wormwood.

Take garden wormwood & lett it dry in the sun untill it be dry
enough to burne, then burne it upon a cleane stone to ashes; take e
ashes and putt them into a stone pott (it must not be an earthen one for
it will penetrate it) then make a stong lye of it with faire water, lett
it stand for two days and two nights after stirring of it in e day; then lett
it setle and poure of the cleare, straining of it untill it is as clear as
Rock-water, then putt the water unto a Chiny or sylver basom & lett
it vapour away upon a soft fire, and there will be remaining a salt
in the bottom; take that salt and weigh it and weigh as much brimstone
beate the brimstone as small as flower and sift it thorow a tifany
then mingle it with the salt & putt them into a Creuset, and putt
the Creustt on a very hott Chearcole fire untill all the brimstone be
evaporated, which you may know by it's leaving vaporing. Then take
out the creusett & when it is cold putt the salt into the Bason again,
and poure faire water upon it, lett it vapour away as before upon
a soft fire. If you have a care of it and not burn it, it will be
exceeding white) you must putt this salt in a very dry glasse
and stop it close with soft wax that no ayre coms to it, for it will
turne into water if ayre coms into it. After this manner you may
make salt of any plante.

A Black Cherries water good for a Surfeit.
or any Stopping in e Stomach or the passion of e heart.

Take 3 pods of black cherries, Bruise them stone & all in a stone mortar
then take Carduus, Balme Mint, sweet marjoran of each one handfull.
3 pints of Claret wine, 4 nuttmeggs & e like quantity of Cinamon.
Beate the Spices and bruise the hearbs & mingle them all together in an
earthen pott, and lett them stand 24 houres to infuse, then still it, and putt
in one grain of musk or ambergris with a litle Suger in e glasses you receive
the water into.

A Receit for the passion of the Heart.

There is a bone in the heart of a Stagg which is to be grated
small and take each morning fasting as much as will laye on a
groate in a spoonfull of this water aforesaid, or of the Syrup
of Cloves, Gillyflower, untill such time as you find yourself better
or if you please to mix some quantity of it in conseve of bitony,
Cowslip, or resemary, and so eate some of that each morning, or
if you like it better, drink it in a spoone; If the distemper doe
much afect the head, this receit is good for it.

You may also take the Intrels of Haire dryed to pouder, but not
the gutts, putt it into an oven after Bread is drawn out, till it
be dry enough to make it into a fine pouder, and when you goes to
Bed, take as much powder as will laye upon a six pence, mingle it
with motherwort made into pouder likewise and putt it into a spoone
and as much black-cherries water as the spoon will hold, and
drink 2 or 3 spoonfull of the water after it.

To make an Excellent Cordial for all passions of
the heart, called Tincture of Balme.

Take a gallon of pure aqua vita twice distilled, or else the Spirit of
wine, putt it into a strong double glasse, t will hold some two gallons,
then putt thereunto 4 handfulls of Balme cleane picked from e stalkes,
half dryed in the shade, lett it stand 8 daies in the sun, e mouth of the
glasse well stopt. Then make ready this pouder. Take Unicorn's hornes
Beases-stone of each the weight of 20 grains, the bone of e hartes harte
8 grains, harts horne & ivory of each the weight of 2 french Crounes.
Read Corall, white Corall and pearle all prepared of each 60 graines,
pure yellow Saunder, Terramenor Terrasamnor Tockmentolor, white amber
and of the horses hoofe of Ireland of each of these 60 grains weight,
Zedoris e weight of 2 grains, pure amber-greece e weight of 10 grains
fine muske e weight of 8 grains and so much of gold leaves. Lett all
these be very well poudred small, putt the pouder to the Balme & lett it
stand 12 daies, every day shaking it. Then take a pound and half of sugar
Candy white made into very fine pouder & dissolve it in a pinte of
read roses water, att a gentle fire, and when it is thorowghly cold, putt
it into the rest of the stuffe & lett it stand other 8 days in the Sunn then
poure out the Clearest in some fine bagg & keepe it in some fine glasse
to your use. the quantity is about a spoonfull when any Body doth sound
or faynt or is subject to Melancholy. In this manner you may make
the tincture







The tincure of Rosemary flowers for all defects of the Braines &

To make poppies water which is good fo a surfeit
Take a quart of small aqua vitor putt it in a glafse & putt good
store of poppies into it cutting of the black ends lett them stand in 2 daies
4hem take them out X some Mary golds flowers of read-
gilly-flowers the flowers of them & lett them stand in a weeke, then take
them out & putt in more. this do till you find them strong of the poppies
and very high coloured, then take some flowers of gelly flowers marigold
and poppies 2 or 3 spriggs of marjoran sweet and a little root of angelica
a good handfull of raisins stoned 6 dates, a little liquorish and anyseed
bruised, half a ounce of white sugar candy, a little hearbe of Rosasolis,-
lett these stand 3 weekes. Then straine it out in a cleanse glafse, you
may putt in fresh aqua vitor to these last hearbes & spices and lett it
stand all the yeare & it will serve for your use.
A Cordiall Water
Take a quarte of Running water, putt to it a pound of lake sugar
mixie them together and lelt them run 3 times thorow a cotton bagg
then putt them a pinte of Cinnamon water, a quarter of minte
of angelica water a quarter of a pinte of Rosasolis, as much of cloves
water, 1 pinte of damask roses water, poure all these 3 times thorow
again then putt in 3 pinted and a half of aqua vitor & poure it-
three times thorow again, clapse it up with musk and amber in your
glapse as you please.
To make the water of the Herbe called Rosasotis, to be-
gathered in the month of June & July.
Take this hearbe Rosasotis (it groweth in meadows or low mash-ground, and
in no other places it is of a faire colour & growes very low & flatt to the ground-
with a main long stalke in the midle with six branches sprining out of the roots round
about the stalke with a leave of faire colour and of meave breath & length) & when
you gather it take heed in any case you touch not the hearbe if self with your hand
for the vertue of the hearbe is then gone, But plucke it up by the stalke & putt it it
a cleane basket. the  leaves of it are full of vertue and strength of nature. Take as-
much of this hearbe as will fill a Bottle put and wash it not in any-wise, take a
Bottle and aqua vitor and putt them both into a vesell, & lett it stand stopt hard
3 days & 3 nights and the 4th dat straine it thorow a cleane linnen cloth in a
cleane glass of pewter pott and putt thereunto a pound of fine sugar beaten small,
half
Physuk and Surgery
half a pound of liquorish beaten small in the fine powder, half a pound of fine dates
the stones pulled out cutt the dates in suies. mingle all this together, stop-
the glasse of pewter bothe that now ayre doe come into it in any wise.
Drink hereof to Red-ward half & spoonfull with ale and as much in the
morning fasting, For there not the weakest man nor woman's body in
the ward that wanteth nature or strength, but it will restore him
again and cause them to be strong & lustie and to have a marvellous
hungry stomach & that thorsty. He that used this 3 times together
shall find great Remedy & comfort hereby and as he feeleth himselfe
so may use it.
This was approved upon one stubbs of westow who being 18 weekes
in a consumption and all and all the physitians in London had given him
over was by drinking hereof 13 times restored unto his strength
and obtained his former health and a good appetite. The making-
hereof before sett downe was delivred unto him by an outlandish man.
Dr. Burgesh's plague water for either men or Beast
Take 3 pintes of Matigos sack and boile a wile, then putt one ounce of minthridate
and one ounce of Teriade a quarter of a pinte of angelica water, Take
of this 3 spoonfull morning & evening if they have already the disease
if not one spoonfull in a morning is suffecient, this is good also for the
falling sickness, small pox, measles & surfeit. But for a Horse if he
be infected you must take a pinte att a time, cloathing him hott & shivering
him to the Belly to make him sweat, next day give him a drench. If you
find that this doe not cure him att once give it him twice for prevention
if he be not infected half the quantity will serve. Men or weomen are
also to sweat when they take it.
For a Burne or Scald
Take Deare swet, May butter Virgin wax & yellow wax of each a like
quantity, set them over a soft fire a melting when they be melted, take
then from the fire & putt in the like quantity of salet ayle as of the rest
snd putt into it a good quantity of snow water beate them all together untill
all the water be beaten out. Note that the snow watermust be made warme
before you putt it to the rest. when you use it spread it upon a soft cloth
& lay it upon the plate. Another.
Beat an oion & bay salt to a plaster so lay it on washe it after the fire is out
with winterseed oyle & water, and scald some lillyes leaves in milk, & apply it.

Palsy Balson M.rs Bodell.

Take 2 quarts of aqua vitor spirit of wine, Infuse in it five
ounces of Juniper-berries grosly bruised, Castorium Inparator each-
half an ounce bruised, 2 handfull of Lavender Spicke Rosmary flowers
steep them with the rest in a pitcher close stopped in a chimny corner
4 days, putting hott Embers round it, stirring it twice a day; then take
6 dragmes of Camphir + put it in a glasse with half a pinte of spirit
of wine, which will dissolve the Camphir, (if you hold the glasse in your
warme hands,) when your pitcher is cold put this in & sett it from the
fire, lett it stand for the space of one houre, then straine it thorow a woolen
bag, and that which coms cleare put by it selfe, the thick when setled you
may putt much cleare from. It is good to anoynt any place that complains
of the palsy, It is good for weakness of the gout + weakness of the sinews
that proceeds from cold humours. A little in a cloth held to the nose is good
for fitts of the mother, good for the head-ach used to the temples. It-
may not be taken inwardly.

to a Botle, then sett it to cool in a pewter dish, then straine it thoron

A Green Salve good for a Cutt or wound bruised
or ulcer if it be not a very foule one.

Take of Rossen a pod of the best turpantine two penny-worth, of-
deer suet finely rendered 6 ounces, of virgins wax a quarter of a pod
of verdegris two penny worth. Melt your rosen wax & suet together
but take heed of burning them; then take them from the fire & stirring
it with a shie put to it presently the turpantine, then gett it on the fire
again stirring it, when it riseth take the verdegris (being first thorowghly
beaten and ground of a painters stone) and mix it with some of the stuff well
then putt it into the Kettle and stirr it all well together & when it riseth -
have ready a gallon or two of spring water and poure it into the water
& worke it well & make it into rolls, & soe lett it laye there 3 days, then
lay it up of use.

For a Cough. My Lord Stanford.

Take one ounce of candied elicampana & one ounce of Syrup of Coltsfoot
and make it into paste with sugar candy & so make it into Lozanges.

A green oyntment good for the scabbs, anguish, swelling of
wounds, Tooth-aches, Bruises, over reaches of Sinews, vaines cramp
stiches either in Man or Beast. It must be made in May
& will last many years being close & cooke kept.

Take read Sage and rue of each one pound, the young Bay-leaves & worm-
wood of each half a pound, pick them but not wash them, cutt them -
small, Beate them long in a faire mortar, then take 3 pods of sheeps
suet hott from the sheepe mince it small & beate it with the hearbs, till
it be of one colour; then putt it all into a faire Bason with a Botle
of the best oyle of olive, worke all together till it becomes a litle soft
then putt it into an earthen pott well stopt for 8 days, then seethe it
in a faire pan with a soft fire, when it is half sodden straine it
into a faire gally-pott putting to it 4 ounces of the oyle of spikie, -
straine it thorow a cleane Canvas, stopp it close with parchement
& leather. You must be carefull of burning of it, therefore drop
2 or 3 drops still in a saucer, when it very green it is enough.
If it be rubbed into the smale of the back it issueth the stone, dropt
into the eares & stopp'd with black wool, helpeth all paine thereof.

For Sore Eyes being troubled with Rheume

Take of hoggs grease very new melt it & when it is cold take 2 ounces
of it & steepe i in Rose-water 6 houres stirring often in the Rose water
after wash it with the best white wine 12 severall times for the space
of six houres more; Then take of Futia well prepared and finely pou-
-dred one ounce of the stone hematises well washed & scraped of allwaies
well washed & made into pouder 12 grains of pearle in pouder 3 grains
mix all together with a litle fennill water & so make it up into oyntment
You must take a litle of this oyntement & putt it on the end of a -
bodkin & putt it in the eyes, and oint the eyes-lidd with a litle of
the same. from Dr Laurentius Sometimes Physician to the french King

For the Piles

Take one handfull of Elder's leaves one of mullet leaves one
of endife, one of Southern wood, one of violets leaves half a handfull
of wormwood as much of marygold leaves cheap all these together
then stamp them then boile them in as much butter unwashed and -
unsalted as will boile them, till the butter be greene, Boile them upon
a soft fire then straine them into a pewter dish & when it is cold melt
it out of the dishe & scrape the bottom of if away, then melt it again &
putt it in an Earthen pott for your use.

Green Oyntment of th Lord Denny
Take 6 pods of May butter unsalted, salett oyle a quarte, hogs-
grease 4 pods, the best rasin & turpentine of each a pod, of franki-
sence 8 ounces, to this quantity, add these hearbs following, smalage
Balme borage, read Sage, Lavender, Cotten, heartgrasse, parsly, confry
called bonisett, Sorrell, Bay leaves, virch leaves long-woote Margoran
and Rosmary of each one handfull, pick all these hearbs, cleane &
wash them, then swing them in a cloth till the water be cleane out,
gather these hearbs after th Sun is risen, then Stamp them in a
stone mortar or wooden, as small as you can. Then take th rosen
& beate it to pouder with your frankicense & melt them first alone
then putt in the butter hogs grease & oyle, and when it is melted-
putt in your hearbs, & lett all boile together a litle while, then take
it from the fire, & lett all boile together a title while, then take
it from the fire & leave not stirring it for a quarter of an houre
when it is of the fire putt in your turpentine & verde greece stirring
it well till it leave boiling, Then putt it into an earthen pott-
stopping it very close and sett i in a Dunghill of horse dung 21 days
then take it up and putt it into a ketle and lett it boile a litle, taking
heed it runs not over, then straine it throrow a course cloth into-
some earthen or gally pott, and when all is strained add to it half
a pod of oyle of spike & keept it close
It is good for all kind of soares, aches in any part of the body,
straynes, Bruises shrinking of sinews.
A Medecine for an Ague.
Take a handfull of rue & two spoonfull of Corrints & a litle-
bay salt, beate them together in a mortar, then putt a little pepper
& safan to it & temper it together & so bind it to both the wrists
for 10 days.

Against the Itche
Take six pennyworth of flower of Brimstone, and as much beaten ginger-
& mix it up with salad oyle and oyle of Spike, of each one equal quantity into
an oyntment, & for 5 or 6 days oynt the plams of your hands & te veins of
your wrists every morning fasting, 7 smell to it & chase is well in; and doe
the same att night when you go to bed. And if it be very bad you mad doe
your armes likewise & this will certainly cure it, But first you must take
as much flower of Brimstone as will laye vpon a shelving in a draught of
new milk for 3 mornings together to drive it out.
For a Fistual Sir Km Digbyes.
Take 4 hundreds of wood worms, sage & rue of each a handfull, Cutt
the hearbs a litte & bruise the worms and putt them into a Canvas bag with
a stene and hang it in 3 gallons of midling Ale when it has done working
and after 3 days drink it and use no other drink in six weekes or till you are
cured.

A Plaster to lay to the feet in a Feauer.
Take 2 handfull of Alehoof. 6 or 8 heads of Garlicks. A handfull

A Cordiall Ale my Lady Osborne.
Take 4 hand full of firr broke in peeces, 3 ounces of Sarsaparilla,
one ounce of China, both slitted; one ounce of harts horne; one once of fery,
ounces of Cosinero roots slitted, 2 ounces of Eringo roots, half an ounce
of winton-bark bruised. Boile all these in 6. gallons of midling Alewort,
iust as it coms from the boiler, before any Yeast be putt to it. When these
haue boiled allmost one houre, straine them out, & sett the liquor a cooling
in Vatts as you doe ordinary drink with Yeast, lett it not be to hott when
You putt Your Yeast to it, The next day tun it vp; Putt into it loose all
all the hearbs you straine it out, a hand full of S..itt, Charoll, 2 handfull
harts tongue dryed, 2 hand full of maidenhaire, Agrimony & liuerwort
of each a hand full, Brookline & water cress of each two hand full; the
Rindes of two Seuill Oranges, half a pod of raisins of the Sun stoned
putt in a gad of steell if you please. Tun all this vp and in 5 or 6. days
drink it constantly if you please.
It is extraordinary good for Vapours or a Consumption, itt revives
the Spiritts & is good against the Scurvey. Botle it in 5. or 6. days.

Ale for the Winds & Stopping in the Stomach my Lady Midlesex.
Take a Rundlett wherein has been white wine, fill it with 7 or 8 gallons
of Strong Ale well brued, and when it has done working putt into the Rundlet
one peck of yellow docks roots, half a peck of Chicory roots, a quarter of
a peck of wood Sorrell's roots, these rootes must be pick'd & wip'd very clean,
& then a litle bruised, which being done putt them into the Rundlet & stopp it.
After 4 or 5 days drink a good Draught euery morning fasting for 7 days
the earlier the better, and att 4 of the clock in the afternoon; And then
You may rest for 2 or 3 days, and take it again so long as it last.

Balsom of Mrs. Yates.
Take Venice turpentine 10 ounces, of Bees wax 8 ounces, storax liquid 6. ounces,

ounce, Ros ewater 3 ounces, Plantin water 2 ounces, Read Sander 5 ounces Suet 12 grains

then take it of the fire and lett it Stand till it be cold, then take it forth of the Skellet &

& straine it thorow a linnen cloth. You must putt to it 12 grains of suet & so lett it coole, putt it
into a gally pott & keep it close stopt for your use. when you make it Keep a Soft fire under it

A polltis for a Soare Throate.
Take Juice of Elder-berries, or the leaves if there be noe. Berries, and

For Swelling or payne in Eyes

Take an apple and roast it, then take the palp of it & putt to it Some read
rose-water, then beate the white of an Egg & mingle the froth of it with
read roses water & palp of the apple, & lay it to the Eyes.

For the fitts of the Mother

Take a penny pott of Muscadine & putt it 2 grains of musk & sett

For the Haw in Eyes

Take 3 cloves beaten fine & searchd 3 spoonfull of fennell water 3 spoonfull
of Malwsey, 3 penny weight of tutia prepared & beaten fine; Mix all these in
a litle glasse & dresse the Eyes with a feather once in 24 houres or oftner as -

For the piles another way.

Take of read Sage, read mint & read plantin of each a like quantity
make water-gruel, when it is almost boiled, shread in your hearbs, when the hearbs
are a little boiled, eate of them without butter or salt if your stomach will beare it,
if not eat it with butter & salt. Some use only oyle of Elders ever houre to ye grief.

A plaster to be applyed to any Joynt that consumes thorough paine.

Take one ounce of Bergamy pitch, 2 ounces of black pitch one ounce of Beeswax,
one ounce of white frankicense, one ounce of Venice turpentine. Item one ounce
of mastic, one of Benjamin, one of Storax half an ounce of Olibanum, these beaten
into fine pouder. Then putt all these into an earthen pipkin & upon Embers, to be
melted temperately together. When you have occasion to use it, spread the plaster
upon the fleshly side of good sheeps leather of such bigness as will go about the -
knees joint, ban & all, & then to be rowled close with a scarlett cloth 3 fingers broad
it will conbinne on without renewing about a fortnight, till it itch & be loose, then
take it of, & with a linnen cloth wipe both the knees & plaster, then ayre the plaster
before the fire, & lay it on again as before, So long as it keeps a sweet sent.

A Purging yet Confortable Broth for Spleen or Liver

Take a knuckle of veale & cutt away all the flesh & fatt, then brake all -
the bones & washe them very cleane, putt it into a pipkin to a gallon of water
and sett it a seething, skum it as long as any thing will arise, then putt -
to it a quarter of a pod of raisins of the sun stones, & half as much-

corrints

Corrints, 2 fennells rootes, 3 parsleys rootes, one siccoreys root, half -

directions above. The oyntment must be used in bed & kept very -

A Medecine for the Collick

Take a quarte of Aqua Vita, half a pod of white sugar candy finely
beaten, 2 ounces of Rubarb beaten small, 2 penny-worth of English -
liquorish shred small, 120 Raisins of the sun stoned, putt all these
into a great glass that it may be more then halfe full & stopp it
close that noe ayre gett in, & soe lett it stand for a month shaking it
every day 3 times from the bottom; and att the month end by such -
time the ingredients will be steepth in the liquor, which may be used -
Viz whensoerver the collick begins to payne, then take one spoonfull
of it, which will make your body soluble & so ease you of your paine
by carrying the winds away; this is good for the winds in ye stomach
and also may be taken before the monts end if there be need, the -
longer it stands the better.

A Medecine against the Stone

First take a glyster to expell winds this done overnight, next -
morning take a quarte of new Milk & putt thereunto a handfull of Ivy
upon the wells, & a handfull of Malloes, a handfull of pellitory of ye wall
shred very small, & when the milk with these hearbs in it is so hott as -
to make a popett, putt into it as much white wine as will curdle it be -
make the popet drink cleare, take of the curd, & hearbes & squeeze into ye
drink 2 or 3 lemons. Drink of this as much as possibly you can & walk
after it. It will expell gravell out of the urinatories & stone out of ye bladder

Physick and Surgery

To make the Countesse of Kent's Pills

Take 2 ounces of the best aloes beaten & searcht, then take Damas roses
half blowme & pickt the best of the leaves, beate them & straine a pinte
of the iuice, & lett it stand a little, then poure the cleare into a Sylver bason
& putt the 2 ounces of Aloes to it, then lett it Stand on the fire to be
Scalding hott, but not boile, then stirr it very well, & when tis thick enough
to make into pills putt it into a gally pott & Keepe it so vntill you vse it,
To a Child you may giue a pill of the bigness of a pea, to a Man or Woman
the bigness of a french beane.

A Medecine to cleare the Eye-sight found out by Dr Purslow
Sometimes Bishop of Hull & Suffragan of York who att the
age of 125 years was able to read any printe without Spectacles
which att the age of 50 he could not.

Take the oyle of the white of an Egg made as followeth; part the white
from the yolk as clear as you can without breaking ye yolk, then beate it
with a broade pointed knive for the space of half an houre in a porringer,
or bason, so the foame will rise as big as a half penny loafe, then smooth it
with a knive like a Sugar loafe, & lett it Stand six houres to setle, then softly
poure out the oyle which will be vnder it into a litle close vial to keep out
the wind or ayre, and the oyle will be as clear as quicksylver, then putt to it
So much running honey as there is of oyle, adding thereunto the Juice of ye
green leafe and roote of the wild dansy as much as of honey, first picking
and well washing it, then Stamping & Straining it thorow a new linnen cloth
and when you have mingled, straine them all again thorow the Same cloth,
then putt it up till it grown cleare. Then putt a drop or two in your Eyes with
a feather morning & evening every day till you find recovery, and afterward
now and then, when you find any decay in your Sight. It will be good to Sleepe
an houre or two after it in the morning if you can.

A Water for the Eyes

Take 3 dragmes of the pouder of Tubia & 3 dragmes of the pouder of allowis
and two dragmes of white sugar candy, six ounces of white wine, Six ounces of
white roses water. Mix all these together & sett them in the chimny corner
in a glass in the winter, and in the Sun in the Summer Shaking twice or
thrice a day.

A Water for a Fistula

Take one ounce of white Coporis, half an ounce of Roach-allum, Burne
both these together 6 penny woth of precipitis finely grounded, to this take
a botle of water, and when it boiled take it of the fire & putt in the ingredients
and when tis cold putt it in Botle, and it will keepe seaven years.

Physick & Surgery

 An Excellent Plaster to Keepe a Woman from Miscarrying.

Take of the choicest mastick 4 dragmes, gum Elemi half an ounce
Burgundy pitch 3 dragmes, Beniamin & Dragon's blood of each two
dragmes, melt all these and Straine them, and add to them of the
bole-armoniak finely poudred two dragmes, of the trochisces called
alipta moschata one dragme, of Venice turpentine a litle boiled
5 dragmes, of the plaster of read led made of the oyle of quinces
half an ounce, of Bees wax one dragme & a half, of Indian balsom
2 Scruples, of oyle of Spike half an ounce. Make these all into
one plaster & spread them vpon leather, one for the region of
the back, and another for the lower region of the Belly.

For the King's Evill

Take the third part of a pound of lignum Vitae, putt to in 4 pintes
of Spring water, putt it all into a new Jugg, paste it very close, & lett it
boile 4 houres in a pott of water. Drinke half a pinte of this in the
morning fasting a little warme, the like quantity in the afternoone,
att 4 of the clock, as much more att going to bed att spring & fall if
to prevent. Butt if there be a Soare then drink it constantly, and lay
a hound's tongue leave on the Soare fresh twice a day.

A Cure for Burston Children

Take of mastick one ounce, of white wax one ounce, of mutton suett
four ounces, melt all these 3 together, lett them not boile but litle,
So dipe in the Cloth & lett it laye till it be cold, then cutt a peece of
the cloth & laye it to the place every 24 houres.
For the pouder. take Kernell of ashine Kees & sleue them in an
Oven and make them into pouder, and giue the party as much of the
pouder as will laye on a 6 pence in any broth or drink they eate
or drinke morning & evening, fasting an houre after it.
Cloath it night & day. Oyntment must be vsed in the morning
before you take the Broth & yn lay halfe an houre after it

To gaine present Ease in fitts of the Stone

Take an ounce of Sweet Almonds newly drawn, an ounce of white Sugar
Candy, or any fine Sugar finely beaten, an ounce of Syrup of Mash-Maloes
Mingle this together, and take half of this proportion att a time, as
often as you find any paine, If you cannot take the syrup you may leave it,
out, I never found this to faile, butt to give present Ease.

To cure the paine or Webs in the Eyes

Take the tops of Hemlock in the Spring, bruise them in a mortar with bay Salt
with half the quantitu as of the other. Apply it to the contrary wrist & bind it close.

Phisick & Surgery.
To cause Speedy Labour.
Take Borax poudred, and give a Scruple or more of it, a dragm finely -
poudred in broath. It looks white if it be right.

A Digestive pouder.
Take Carroway seeds, sweet fennell seeds of each half an ounce. Ginger &
mace of each a quarter of an ounce, sweet almonds 2 ounces; the Kernells of
pine-nutts and pistaches of each one ounce, Lemons, Citrons & Oranges candied
of each 2 ounces, English liquorish & Naples bisketts of each 6 ounces, Cinamon
a dragme, beate all these together & mingle them. Eate thereof att yr pleasure

For an Ague.
Take London treacle & spread it on a plaster, and lay it very large upon
the stomack & top of the belly, and take 2 or 3 hours before the fitt coms a litle
draught of posset drinck that has had some hearts horne & Cloves gelly flowers -
boiled in it. This has cured a Quartan. You must drinck the posset every houre
for Some time before the fitt.

To Recover the Colour after an Ague or Sickness.
Take Rubarbe Sliced Veru Small & some 9 raisins of the Sun Stoned & dipt -
into the rubarbe & so take it fasting every morning for a weeke.

To purge.
Take 15 grains of Jallop & infuse it over night in white wine & Cinamon.

For a Cough.
Take 20 tops of Rosmary & so much hysop & a quarte of sack, putt it in a -
botle & stopp it, and So boile it in a pott of water, till it be consumed half away, then
take it up and putt in as much honey as will sweeten it, then boile it to a syrup-
and take 3 spoonfull att night.

Plaster of Dr. Knowlles.
Take 2 pods of Oyle of Olive, one pods of read lead, one pod of white lead,
Very well calmed to dust, 12 ounces of Spanish Soape, Incorporate all them toge=
=ther in an earthen pipkins well glazed before you boile it; if it be enough the
Soape will com uppermost. Then putt it upon a small fire of coales continuing the
Same fire for an houre, still stirring it with a stick; then make the fire Something
bigger till the readness turne towards a gray collour, & so till it be the collour -
of oyle you must keepe it stirring; then drop it on the table, and if it doth not
Stich upon that or your fingers it is enough' then poure it on a oyled board, &-
rowle it into rowles. It will last many yeares. the older the better.
Being layd on the Stomack it procures appetite & easeth the paines. Layd on ye
Belly it cures the Collick. Laid on the Rains, it is good for the bloody flux, the runing
of the rains, the heat of the back, but for a hott back putt not So much read lead. It-
helps all aches & breakes felons & pushes & other impostumes & healeth them. It -
draws out any running humors, without breaking the skin. It helps all diseases of the
fundament layd to it. Laid to the head it is good for the Eyes. Laid to the belly it causes
the termes & makes apt to conseption. It is good for all Sores.
